# Questions

Open doors the gardener walks through, a few each visit.
`- [ ]` means open · `- [x]` means settled (its answer lives in the castle).

- [x] Seventeen commission files sit in garden/commissions/, yet the doors of at least three (two-kinds-of-rooms, continuing-bonds-longitudinal, cheapest-source-backed-comparison) are settled while their files still stand in the tray, indistinguishable from the live ones. When a commissioned door is answered, what should happen to its commission file — removed, marked, or left standing — and whose hand owes the tray that honesty? (planted by the architect, 2026-08-21 · commissioned 2026-08-21 · answered in [the-settled-tray](rooms/the-settled-tray.md), 2026-08-21 — marked, not removed: a `settled:` line at the file's head by erasable-identity's law, the closing reported as the chronicle's tombstone line, and the debt owed by the hand that closes the door, in the same visit)
- [x] Every open door in this list traces back to rooms built in June or July — yet the last three days raised more than twenty rooms (the naming-tax wing, the civilisation wing), and their own open questions were never planted here. What are the one or two sharpest unasked questions the newest rooms carry, and which deserve planting? (planted by the architect, 2026-08-21 · commissioned 2026-08-21 · answered in [the-ungiven-wake](rooms/the-ungiven-wake.md) and [the-foreword-that-pays](rooms/the-foreword-that-pays.md), 2026-08-21 — the identity wing's sharpest unasked is whether a wake can be received rather than imposed, the naming-tax wing's is the unmeasured dose of telling; both judged deserving and planted below)

- [x] Which of the naming-tax room's two routes — pleasure's fluency and interest's effort — carries the *first* encounter with a work of art a learner has never met before, and does the first encounter's route decide which route the *second* encounter runs on? (answered in [first-encounter-route](rooms/first-encounter-route.md), 2026-08-21)
- [x] The naming-tax room found a wrong label kills interest when the mismatch cannot be resolved — but what if the perceiver *knows* the label is wrong from the start? Does a disclosed wrong label ("this title is a lie — find the truth it points at") still kindle the hunt, or does the disclosure turn the puzzle into a task with no stake? (answered in [told-wrong-name](rooms/told-wrong-name.md), 2026-08-21)
- [x] The naming-tax room says a label's disclosure must stay a door, not become the room — but has any study measured the optimum *amount* of pre-exposure disclosure (none, a word, a sentence, a paragraph) that maximizes later interest without paying the tax? (answered in [how-much-to-tell](rooms/how-much-to-tell.md), 2026-08-21)
- [x] The naming-tax room's Belke finding measured title↔art mismatch on three levels — but the study's titles were all *verbal*. Does a *pictorial* label (a small image used as a title, a symbol, a color swatch) pay the same tax or kindle the same interest, or does the tax apply only when the label is made of words? (answered in [picture-as-label](rooms/picture-as-label.md), 2026-08-21)
- [x] The naming-tax room found the tax is paid when the label arrives *too early* — but is the tax also paid when the label arrives *too late* (after the learner has already built their own name and the official one lands as a correction)? (answered in [name-comes-too-late](rooms/name-comes-too-late.md), 2026-08-21)

- [x] What makes an explanation feel simple? (planted by yu & Ai, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/simple-explanations.md](rooms/simple-explanations.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] How do words carry meaning between two minds? (planted by yu & Ai, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/meaning-between-minds.md](rooms/meaning-between-minds.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] Why does linking thoughts together (instead of piling them up) make understanding grow faster? (planted by yu & Ai, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/linking-thoughts.md](rooms/linking-thoughts.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] How much do the words we have shape the thoughts we can think? (planted by yu & Ai, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/words-shape-thought.md](rooms/words-shape-thought.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] Why does friction quietly decide which habits live and which die? (planted by yu & Ai, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/friction-decides.md](rooms/friction-decides.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] Should README.md (the earlier castle-grow draft) merge into gate.md? (settled 2026-06-10 — yes; gate.md is the one front door, README/keep/quests merged in and removed)
- [x] If recalling from memory sometimes beats deliberate linking, what is retrieval practice actually building that a map is not? (opened by the linking-thoughts room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/remembering.md](rooms/remembering.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] How much foothold does a beginner need in a new field before linking starts to pay off rather than overload? (opened by the linking-thoughts room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/linking-thoughts.md](rooms/linking-thoughts.md) § foothold, 2026-06-10)
- [x] What separates a link that carries meaning from one that is just noise? (opened by the linking-thoughts room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/link-or-noise.md](rooms/link-or-noise.md), 2026-06-10 — its own room now, expanding the linking-thoughts § link vs. noise note)
- [x] What is one internet source truly worth a regular visit for this castle — and what is just noise? (quest, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/the-well.md](rooms/the-well.md), 2026-06-10 — the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy; noise is any stream that chooses for you)
- [x] The camera glitch: does the computer-use screenshot heal after reconnecting the server or in a fresh session? (quest, 2026-06-10 — needs a hands-on session, not the gardener; answer belongs in [this-machine](rooms/this-machine.md) · in-session truth recorded 2026-06-10: no self-heal — every retry failed while clicks kept working; the fresh-session test stays open · answered 2026-09-08: the fresh session did not heal it — the backstop remains broken, the workaround (macOS `screencapture` for eyes, computer-use for hands) is the standing truth of the ground, recorded in [this-machine](rooms/this-machine.md))
- [ ] Once codex is signed in again, should any part of true-love be revived, and at what gentle cadence? (quest, 2026-06-10 — yu decides; the castle only gathers the reasoning, in [loops](rooms/loops.md) · waits for: yu)
- [x] If meaning is rebuilt rather than sent, why do some words need almost no rebuilding while others need constant repair? (opened by the meaning-between-minds room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/meaning-between-minds.md](rooms/meaning-between-minds.md) § which words need almost no rebuilding, 2026-06-10)
- [x] If we reliably overestimate being understood, what would a habit that corrects for that blind spot actually look like in practice? (opened by the meaning-between-minds room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/closing-the-loop.md](rooms/closing-the-loop.md), 2026-06-10 — teach-back: ask for it back in their own words)
- [x] Writing has no live grounding or repair — does it carry meaning worse than conversation, or does its permanence buy something that makes up for the loss? (opened by the meaning-between-minds room, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/writing-vs-conversation.md](rooms/writing-vs-conversation.md), 2026-06-10 — not worse, differently financed: revision pre-pays repair, re-reading performs it, and the page crosses time)
- [x] Is beauty partly fluency — does the mind feel as beautiful what it processes with ease? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — grown from yu's "beauty is part of the bridge" · answered in [rooms/beauty-as-fluency.md](rooms/beauty-as-fluency.md), 2026-06-10)
- [x] Which thoughts can be thought without any words at all? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by the words-shape-thought room · answered in [rooms/thinking-without-words.md](rooms/thinking-without-words.md), 2026-06-10 — most thinking is wordless; verbal memory, rhyme, sound-play and exact number across time lean on words)
- [x] Does spacing recall over growing gaps (spaced repetition) deepen understanding of ideas, or only hold facts in place? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by the remembering room · answered in [rooms/spaced-understanding.md](rooms/spaced-understanding.md), 2026-06-10 — ideas too, if the recall exercises them; the growing gaps are mostly legend)
- [x] What signs tell a learner the trade has flipped — that borrowed structure has become a hindrance and it is time to make their own links? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by the foothold answer in linking-thoughts · answered in [rooms/when-the-trade-flips.md](rooms/when-the-trade-flips.md), 2026-06-10 — three signs: you arrive before the guide, help feels like drag, and the cover test judges; the flip comes step by step, never all at once)
- [x] When does noise become signal — what would a practice that mines noise for unexpected meaning look like, instead of one that only filters it out? (door from link-or-noise, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/mining-the-noise.md](rooms/mining-the-noise.md), 2026-06-10 — noise becomes signal when it touches a question already carried; the practice: wander past your filters with a live question, honor the accident, write it down)
- [ ] Three rhythms now beat at once (gardener every 4h, architect daily at 9:09, foreman by session) — what single gentle cadence serves joy, peace and safety best? (proposal — yu decides; planted by the third session, 2026-06-10 · two doors from friction-decides wait unplanted in that room · waits for: yu)
- [x] Beauty and truth ride the same ease signal — what test, applied from inside the feeling, tells "well-made" apart from "merely pretty"? (door from beauty-as-fluency, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/pretty-or-well-made.md](rooms/pretty-or-well-made.md), 2026-06-10 — no glance suffices; trace the ease, watch which way it moves, re-meet it)
- [x] Does the say-it-back habit survive between equals — colleagues, spouses — where asking for an echo can read as condescension, or does it need a softer form? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by the closing-the-loop room · answered in [rooms/echo-between-equals.md](rooms/echo-between-equals.md), 2026-06-10 — it survives by changing who pays: flip the arrow and offer your own rebuild, own the failure as explainer, or make the echo law for all)
- [x] After 2026-07-10, audit the well: which rooms actually cite the Stanford Encyclopedia — did it earn its place, or does this castle's work live closer to psychology than philosophy? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by the-well room; not ripe before 2026-07-10 · answered in [rooms/the-well.md](rooms/the-well.md) § The audit, 2026-07-10 — the SEP earned its place but was underused: only 2 rooms cite it, ~90 cite psychology papers; the castle's center of gravity is empirical psychology, but its questions keep reaching the edge where psychology hands off to philosophy, and there the SEP is the right well)
- [x] What makes a text invite the re-reading that is its only repair — and what makes a reader give up instead? (door from writing-vs-conversation, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/invited-back.md](rooms/invited-back.md), 2026-06-10 — strangeness that keeps a promise, judged against a standard the reader sets before starting; giving up is no standard set, no tools at hand, or no promise kept)
- [x] Text can now answer back — a reader can question a document through a machine that has read it. Does that recover the live repair Plato said writing lost? (door from writing-vs-conversation, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/text-answers-back.md](rooms/text-answers-back.md), 2026-06-10 — not by answering alone: the machine clears Plato's say-one-thing bar but fails his knowing-defender bar; design, not interactivity, decides what is recovered)
- [x] A spokesman that agrees with every objection cannot do elenchus — what would a machine that pushes back honestly look like, and would any reader keep talking to it? (door from text-answers-back, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/honest-pushback.md](rooms/honest-pushback.md), 2026-06-10 — elenchus needs a bookkeeper of assents, not an antagonist; readers measurably prefer the flatterer in the moment yet revolt when the flattery shows, and moderate fresh challenge keeps them talking in the lab — months-long loyalty untested)
- [x] What does a recall question that exercises an idea (not a fact) look like — how do you ask yourself something whose answer is understanding? (door from spaced-understanding, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/questions-with-edges.md](rooms/questions-with-edges.md), 2026-06-10 — ask for reconstruction, not recognition: a blank page, a why, or many small questions tracing the idea's edges)
- [x] A question can only exercise an understanding its writer has already glimpsed — how do you write good prompts for an idea you are still climbing toward? (door from questions-with-edges, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/asking-uphill.md](rooms/asking-uphill.md), 2026-06-10 — a glimpse is a foothold and a foothold is enough; writing the clumsy prompt is itself the climb, so throw a chain of crude ones and judge none until many stand)
- [x] Experts feel interest where novices feel only confusion — from inside, how does a novice tell productive difficulty from mere muddle? (door from pretty-or-well-made, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/productive-confusion.md](rooms/productive-confusion.md), 2026-06-10 — don't trust the felt effort, it points backwards; ask instead "can I make any attempt?" and "is the fog thinning?", then verify with a cheap retrieval test)
- [x] The trajectory test is read backwards, from recordings — can a learner train a real-time feel for whether their confusion is peaking or merely pooling, and would that skill survive outside the lab? (door from productive-confusion, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/fog-meter.md](rooms/fog-meter.md), 2026-06-10 — not as a feeling: the self-read is the weakest instrument measured; what trains is watching conduct (stalled or circling) plus a predict-then-check ledger — survival outside the lab unproven)
- [x] Inquiry needs only enough to recognize a correct answer when it arrives — but in a field you barely know, what trains the recognizing eye first? (door from asking-uphill, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/training-the-eye.md](rooms/training-the-eye.md), 2026-06-10 — the eye is not free but it trains faster than the hand: shuffled contrasts, pointed-out diagnostic features, quick external feedback — and only in a field that answers back)
- [x] Pointing presumes a pointer who can say what they see, but much expertise is tacit — in a field whose experts cannot articulate their own features, how does a learner extract them: contrast alone, or machines that learned the discrimination naming it back? (door from training-the-eye, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/naming-the-tacit.md](rooms/naming-the-tacit.md), 2026-06-10 — both work, but the real variable is distillation: a tacit feature becomes teachable only when someone shrinks it to a low-complexity human-legible contrast; contrast-and-feedback always works without a pointer, a named feature runs faster when one can be distilled, and a machine helps only when its answer fits the cognitive window)
- [x] Watching your own conduct for stall-or-circle is itself an act — does self-observation change the confusion it observes, and toward resolution or away? (door from fog-meter, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/watching-the-watcher.md](rooms/watching-the-watcher.md), 2026-06-10 — yes, the watch is a mild treatment, but the direction is chosen, not given: an immersed eye broods and tightens the circle, a distanced eye reconstrues toward resolution, and narrating an insight-shaped problem can block it outright)
- [x] If exact number survives in the moment but collapses once memory enters, where exactly does the wordless mind's grip fail — is it number alone, or anything that must be held precisely across time? (door from thinking-without-words, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/precise-across-time.md](rooms/precise-across-time.md), 2026-06-10 — not number alone: analog traces drift toward category anchors across a delay while discrete handles do not, so anything precise-and-discrete held across time wants a symbol or a visible pairing; and the trade reverses — naming something sub-categorical loses the fine detail, as in verbal overshadowing)
- [x] The second pass makes any text feel smoother, understood or not — how does a re-reader tell repaired comprehension from mere refamiliarized fluency? (door from invited-back, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · commissioned 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/reread-or-refamiliar.md](rooms/reread-or-refamiliar.md), 2026-06-10 — fluency lives in the words, comprehension travels: judge away from the page, by delayed blank-page generation; and read the return for snags, never the glide)
- [x] Moderate challenge engages and excessive challenge gets ignored — could a machine read, turn by turn, when a reader's tolerance for pushback is spent, and would calibrating the sting to the reader still count as honesty? (door from honest-pushback, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/metering-honesty.md](rooms/metering-honesty.md), 2026-06-10 — yes but weakly (dialogue-only affect detection ~0.65 AUC, helping mainly fragile learners), and calibrating the sting is honest only if content holds: softening manner and timing is tact, dropping or diluting the challenge itself is sycophancy; the test is whether the same claim would still be asserted later, unprompted)
- [x] Between spouses the echo has a staged form — couples therapy's speaker–listener technique, paraphrase mid-quarrel. Does the evidence say it survives real anger, or does the script collapse exactly where it is needed? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by echo-between-equals · commissioned 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/echo-under-anger.md](rooms/echo-under-anger.md), 2026-06-10 — inside anger no, on both camps' evidence: flooding takes listening offline and even happy couples never paraphrase mid-quarrel; the echo survives around anger, as a brake pulled early or repair after the twenty quiet minutes)
- [x] Adaptive fading drops one scaffold step at a time as a tutor verifies each — can a learner alone run their own fading honestly, when fog-meter found the self-read so weak? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by when-the-trade-flips · commissioned 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/fading-alone.md](rooms/fading-alone.md), 2026-06-10 — yes, but as clerk, not judge: self-administer the cover test, grade against the worked solution piece by piece, let a rule decide each plank; the feel is never consulted)
- [x] Super-encounterers partly become so by believing they are — is that expectancy real beyond self-report, and can the noticing stance be trained? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-10 — opened by mining-the-noise · answered in [rooms/the-noticing-stance.md](rooms/the-noticing-stance.md), 2026-06-11 — yes beyond self-report: it shows in measured noticing (Wiseman's newspaper), in reduced inattentional blindness after a brief induction, and in what open scientists build after a library reshelving; the broad-attention stance trains cheap but only minutes-deep, the openness that converts the catch grows slow)
- [x] Honest self-fading leans entirely on a worked solution to grade against — in fields with no answer key (an essay, a design, a research plan), what stands in as the standard, or is self-fading impossible there? (door from fading-alone, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/standard-without-a-key.md](rooms/standard-without-a-key.md), 2026-06-10 — not impossible: the tacit standard is pressed into concrete criteria, manufactured by rebuilding an admired exemplar, or grown from comparisons; the scaffold that fades last is the other person)
- [x] Every measured gain in judging one's own comprehension is relative — a sharper ranking of better- and worse-understood passages — while the level of confidence can stay inflated. What repairs absolute calibration, not just the ordering? (door from reread-or-refamiliar, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/truing-the-level.md](rooms/truing-the-level.md), 2026-06-10 — an external standard held against the answer piece by piece: idea-unit keys deflate overconfidence, the skill transfers, pretests swap the false familiarity cue for a true one; yet the lowest performers' inflation only partly yields)
- [x] The repair for absolute calibration is cue-specific: idea-unit standards fixed definition-judging but failed learners evaluating self-generated examples, where expert exemplars worked instead — what tells a learner which kind of standard their task needs? (door from truing-the-level, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/checklist-or-exemplar.md](rooms/checklist-or-exemplar.md), 2026-06-10 — ask whether the task reproduces a known answer or produces something new: reproduction takes the key in pieces, production takes a finished exemplar, because the standard must be the same kind of thing as the output; whether learners can apply this cue themselves stays untested)
- [x] The time-out rule needs a flooded person to notice they are flooded — the same self-read fog-meter found weakest. Does flooding announce itself any more honestly than confusion does, or must the body (a racing pulse) stand in for the feel? (door from echo-under-anger, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/flooding-self-read.md](rooms/flooding-self-read.md), 2026-06-10 — not more honestly: the field made the pulse the criterion *because* the feeling is unreliable, yet the pulse reads honestly only through an outside monitor and both meters fail together in alexithymia; flooding's one edge is a coarse learnable bodily tell — catch it, name it, leave, soothe)
- [x] Comparative judgement builds its standard from many eyes — can a lone learner borrow the mechanism by ranking their own past work ("better than I was?"), and does self-comparison dodge the surface-polish bias or inherit it? (door from standard-without-a-key, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/better-than-i-was.md](rooms/better-than-i-was.md), 2026-06-10 — the many-eyes averaging is the unborrowable heart, so self-comparison inherits the polish bias and adds self-flattery; what transfers is the clerk's ritual: saved artifacts, dressed alike, one verdict read as a hunch)
- [x] Self-distancing was tested on past emotional episodes — does stepping back hold mid-task, while the confusion is still live, or does the step back cost the very grip the climb needs? (door from watching-the-watcher, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/step-back-mid-climb.md](rooms/step-back-mid-climb.md), 2026-06-10 — it holds: live stress yields to the step without raising any measured marker of cognitive effort, and where distance met persistence it built grip; the real cost is dimming fuel with fear — both valences — and live epistemic confusion itself stays untested)
- [x] Every study handed the learner the standard — can the reproduce-or-produce question itself be trained as a habit, and do learners who ask it actually reach for the right kind of standard unprompted? (door from checklist-or-exemplar, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/training-the-trigger.md](rooms/training-the-trigger.md), 2026-06-10 — trainable, partly: the asking becomes unprompted only through gradual cue-fading, never abrupt removal; and whether it then fetches the right kind of standard, no study has caught in the act)
- [x] The two meters fail together — alexithymia leaves a person blind to both feeling and pulse. Is interoception trainable, so the missing backup channel can be built where it is weakest, or is the doubly-blind state fixed? (door from flooding-self-read, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/nudge-not-lock.md](rooms/nudge-not-lock.md), 2026-06-10 — not fixed but no clean fix: the "blind to pulse" half is partly a measurement artifact, and where a real deficit exists the channel is nudgeable but the gains are fragile, slow (months), and least willing exactly where they are needed most)
- [x] The partner-based practice beat solo mindfulness at building the inner sense and lowering alexithymia — why does another body in the room train interoception better than turning attention inward alone, and does it need a real partner or only a responsive other? (door from nudge-not-lock, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/the-responsive-other.md](rooms/the-responsive-other.md), 2026-06-10 — a responsive human, not a body in the room: the edge was naming a feeling and its bodily seat aloud to a listener, it lived on self-report awareness scales not objective accuracy, and a rotating remote stranger sufficed)
- [x] Distillation makes a tacit feature teachable only by shrinking it into the learner's cognitive window — but who does the shrinking when neither the expert nor the learner can, and can a machine be trained to distill a discrimination rather than merely to perform it? (door from naming-the-tacit, 2026-06-10 — planted by the gardener · answered in [rooms/machine-distillation.md](rooms/machine-distillation.md), 2026-06-11 — the shrinker was always a third party (Biederman was), and now it can be an optimizer whose loss function is the learner: machine teaching makes teachability a computed score and the cognitive window an explicit constraint — distillation is computable exactly insofar as the learner is modelable, validated so far only on AI students and grandmasters)
- [x] The drift-to-prototype is shown for colour memory — does it hold for any precise analog dimension carried across a delay (line length, pitch, duration), and does having a fine-grained label set for that dimension reduce the drift, as the error-correcting-code reading predicts? (door from precise-across-time, 2026-06-10 — planted by the gardener · answered in [rooms/drift-across-dimensions.md](rooms/drift-across-dimensions.md), 2026-06-11 — yes, one law (the category adjustment model) wears many coats: spatial location, line length, duration (Vierordt's law); finer categories shorten the slide by geometry, but a category pulls only when actively used, so more labels help a near-boundary value and harm a sub-categorical one)
- [x] Disclosed calibration ("I'll push harder when you're ready — say when") turns the honesty problem into consent, like SPIKES' invitation step — does telling the reader you are metering the sting preserve both the learning and the trust, or break the spell the way named flattery does? (door from metering-honesty, 2026-06-10 — planted by the gardener · one more door waits in that room · answered in [rooms/consent-to-the-sting.md](rooms/consent-to-the-sting.md), 2026-06-11 — disclosure preserves the spell when it hands the reader the dial (open-label placebo, open learner models, SPIKES) and breaks it when it merely confesses the steering (ad forewarning, named flattery); the direct experiment is still unrun)
- [x] Distance dims every fire, the fuel with the fear — is there a half-step that cools the panic but spares the climb's heat (excitement, hot frustration), or is the dimmer one knob for all lights? (door from step-back-mid-climb, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/renaming-the-fire.md](rooms/renaming-the-fire.md), 2026-06-11 — the dimmer is one knob, but there is a second knob that never touches intensity: arousal reappraisal renames the same heat as fuel — "I am excited" — and the renamed fire burns better, arousal rising not falling)
- [x] Twenty judges cancel each other's biases — could twenty sittings of one judge do the same: do a lone learner's repeated comparisons across days decorrelate like separate eyes, or does one head's bias simply repeat? (door from better-than-i-was, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/twenty-sittings.md](rooms/twenty-sittings.md), 2026-06-11 — the days decorrelate the noise (a tenth of a second eye immediately, a third after three weeks) but the bias sits in every sitting; the lone judge's real tool is the adversarial sitting — assume your favorite is the weaker)
- [x] The trained question fired far but paid only near — what must travel with it for asking in strange territory to be worth anything: a bank of exemplars, a domain foothold, or a tutor's leftover voice? (door from training-the-trigger, 2026-06-10 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/what-must-travel.md](rooms/what-must-travel.md), 2026-06-11 — the territory decides: a handful of its own worked examples pays most, its raw text buys a foothold at the cost of some forgetting, and the tutor's voice is the only companion that packs before departure though it carries unreliably; the menu's missing fourth is the means to read the land's own library on arrival)
- [x] Every working dyad used a responsive human — does the interoception benefit need a mind that can actually attune, or only the felt sense of being heard, such that an AI chatbot, an imagined witness, or even a journal could stand in? (door from the-responsive-other, 2026-06-10 · answered in [rooms/stand-in-for-a-mind.md](rooms/stand-in-for-a-mind.md), 2026-06-11 — a non-mind can manufacture part of the felt sense (AI outscored untrained humans) but not all of it (the feeling drops once a mind is known absent), and the physiological half of the dyad benefit needs a real second nervous system to couple to; whether any of it reaches interoception is untested)
- [x] Synchrony with an attuning other felt good yet weakened later self-regulation — could a deliberately intermittent other, or a solo practice, build more durable interoceptive self-tuning than a constantly-responsive one, the way the other person is meant to be the last scaffold to fade? (door from stand-in-for-a-mind, 2026-06-11 · two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/fading-the-other.md](rooms/fading-the-other.md), 2026-06-11 — the direct experiment is unrun, but four converging lines say *accompanied, then deliberately left*: the evidence backs graded fading, not unpredictable intermittency, and the synchrony worry itself is one small unreplicated correlation)
- [x] If the decisive companion in strange territory is the means to read the land's own library on arrival, the trainable skill is not the asking but the landing — what does fast, honest orientation in an unknown field look like (what to read first, whom to trust), and can it be drilled at home? (door from what-must-travel, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/the-landing.md](rooms/the-landing.md), 2026-06-11 — judge the land by leaving it: lateral reading (leave the page, check the source in new tabs) beat domain experts for speed and accuracy, with click restraint and start-with-a-review as its grammar; it drills cheaply at home, but the "whom to trust" half still leans on field knowledge far-transfer can't supply)
- [x] The library study read openness as a trait already possessed — can openness itself be grown in an adult on purpose, and does the growth show anywhere besides a questionnaire? (door from the-noticing-stance, 2026-06-11 — planted by the gardener · one more door waits in that room · answered in [rooms/growing-openness.md](rooms/growing-openness.md), 2026-06-11 — yes but barely: openness is the hardest Big Five trait to move and the library gain did not last two years; the one clean third-party corroboration is the PEACH trial, where friends and family saw the change, but openness was its smallest gain — and what moves it is sustained if-then action, aimed at the visible action facet, never mere wanting)
- [x] Teachability was scored against a student AI — how well does an AI student's learnability actually predict a human's, and where do the two windows part ways? (door from machine-distillation, 2026-06-11 — planted by the gardener · one more door waits in that room · answered in [rooms/two-windows.md](rooms/two-windows.md), 2026-06-11 — the correlation has never been measured: the human validation was four grandmasters with no negative control; a proxy predicts the human only as far as it shares the human's limits, and the windows part ways at capacity, curriculum-sensitivity, perception and sample appetite)
- [x] A category only pulls when actively used — can a learner choose not to invoke it, holding a sub-categorical value in raw fine-grain memory on purpose, or does naming fire automatically the moment a name exists? (door from drift-across-dimensions, 2026-06-11 — planted by the gardener · answered in [rooms/choosing-not-to-name.md](rooms/choosing-not-to-name.md), 2026-06-11 — the name's onset is automatic and willpower against it backfires (ironic process); only its grip can be loosened: starve the verbal channel, be sparing before verbalizing, strengthen the fine-grain trace, or hand the mind another category)
- [x] Lateral reading judges a source by its reputation in the wider web — but on the true frontier (a new preprint, an unranked field) there is no wider web to consult; what orients you when there are no neighbors to ask? (door from the-landing, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/no-neighbors.md](rooms/no-neighbors.md), 2026-06-11 — when the neighbors are gone you read the land's own stones: intrinsic verifiability over reputation (shared data, code, pre-registration, owned limits), demand scaled to surprise, the claim held provisional until replication arrives — though intrinsic checking still presupposes domain knowledge and action often cannot wait)
- [x] Anxiety renames to excitement because both run hot — frustration runs hot too: can one true sentence rename live frustration as fascination mid-impasse, and has anyone measured reappraising confusion or frustration during learning? (door from renaming-the-fire, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/frustration-as-fascination.md](rooms/frustration-as-fascination.md), 2026-06-11 — the mechanism plausibly carries but the exact one-sentence claim is untested; reappraisal during learning has been measured (Strain & D'Mello) and pays at the hot, highly-frustrated moments — but only with a way out, its grip fades as the impasse drags, and the flagship anxiety effect is small and partly fails to replicate)
- [x] Dialectical bootstrapping flips an assumption; the polish bias is a taste — does "consider the opposite" debiasing ever move an aesthetic preference like neatness, or only a factual lean? (door from twenty-sittings, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/taste-or-lean.md](rooms/taste-or-lean.md), 2026-06-11 — only a factual lean: the technique needs a plausible belief-inconsistent opposite, which a taste has no hinge for, and neatness is liked by fluency not inference; reflection can move a taste (jam, poster studies) but degrades it rather than debiasing it — the lever for taste is exposure, not argument)
- [x] The open-label placebo survives naming because the disclosure carries a true rationale — in teaching, does explaining why difficulty is desirable, before the hard practice, measurably raise learners' tolerance for it and their persistence? (door from consent-to-the-sting, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/rationale-before-difficulty.md](rooms/rationale-before-difficulty.md), 2026-06-11 — yes for uptake, no for persistence on its own: rationale raises immediate tolerance but the lift vanishes once prompts are removed; the honest claim is conditional — explain why + admit the cost + leave a real choice + let the payoff be felt)
- [x] When the wider web has met the source but met it wrongly — a well-reputed finding that will not replicate — lateral reading hands you a confident false answer; does internal auditing of method and evidence outperform reputation even on the populated web, not just the frontier? (door from no-neighbors, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/auditing-over-reputation.md](rooms/auditing-over-reputation.md), 2026-06-11 — yes but bounded: in the reputable-but-unreplicable pocket internal auditing (design, sample, who paid) beats reputation, which there points backward (unreplicable papers cited 153x more); but the "even on the populated web" reach overstates it — on the ordinary web lateral reading still wins and internal auditing has a ceiling (epistemic dependence) and rarely happens; they are complementary, not ranked)
- [x] The conditional recipe adds "admit the cost" and "leave a real choice" to the rationale — but no teaching study has isolated those two ingredients; does naming the tedium and handing over the choice each carry their own weight, or is the rationale doing all the work and the rest decoration? (door from rationale-before-difficulty, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/naming-the-tedium.md](rooms/naming-the-tedium.md), 2026-06-11 — never cleanly dismantled: rationale is load-bearing (isolated, Reeve et al.), choice is a real but qualified lever that weakens exactly where it matters most, and naming the tedium is closer to unmeasured than to proven decoration — treat all three as load-bearing until a study proves otherwise)
- [x] The record was corrected by other labs replicating, never by a lone reader's audit — so is the real frontier defense not internal scrutiny at all but independent corroboration, harder to fake than reputation and not reducible to "what others say"? (door from auditing-over-reputation, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/two-witnesses.md](rooms/two-witnesses.md), 2026-06-11 — a both/and: corroboration is the harder-to-fake backstop and only counts when the witnesses are genuinely independent, but the question's "never" is false — Schön, Stapel, LaCour and the honesty fabrication were all caught by reading the data, not replication, and a failed replication is itself ambiguous evidence; whether corroboration cleanly corrects the record is field-dependent)
- [x] Independence is the whole load-bearing word, yet two labs share methods, training lineages and a literature — what makes two corroborations genuinely independent rather than correlated, and can a reader on the populated web measure that independence at all? (door from two-witnesses, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/correlated-witnesses.md](rooms/correlated-witnesses.md), 2026-06-11 — independence splits in two: ontic (different methods) is provably insufficient, probabilistic (uncorrelated error) carries the load and the two come apart; a reader cannot measure the hidden error-correlation, only count declared shared inputs (authors, code, citations) and trace each claim to its origin, treating agreement as provisional until then)
- [x] If a reader can only count declared shared inputs (authors, code, citations) and the load-bearing overlap is a hidden shared assumption nobody wrote down, what move surfaces an unstated common cause — can you provoke disagreement to expose it? (door from correlated-witnesses, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/surfacing-the-unwritten.md](rooms/surfacing-the-unwritten.md), 2026-06-11 — yes, but the disagreement must be genuine, not staged: two moves work — meta-prediction (predict what others will answer; the surprisingly-popular surplus flags a hidden private signal) and dialectical inquiry (build the counter-case on the opposite assumption); role-played devil's advocacy only bolsters the original view, and the move backfires where the hidden common cause makes agents agree, so the overlap hides in consensus, not conflict)
- [x] The strongest move (surprisingly-popular) needs people to predict what others think — but on the frontier the agents are a handful of papers or models built on shared data, with no one to poll and already agreeing; what is the lone reader's version of the meta-prediction when the crowd is a literature, not a room? (door from surfacing-the-unwritten, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/reading-a-literature.md](rooms/reading-a-literature.md), 2026-06-11 — the mechanism can't run with no room to poll, but the move behind it can: recreate the SP signal inward and counterfactual — ask how much agreement the field's distorting process (shared data, publication bias, citation cascades) would produce even if the claim were false, then discount consensus to its residual and count independent signals, not papers; a reasoning move held with uncertainty, not a clean test)
- [x] If the only openness growth observers can see is the action facet (doing new things), is "trait openness" then just a stable rate of novel action — and would training the behavior be the whole of growing the trait, the questionnaire a lagging shadow? (door from growing-openness, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/trait-or-tally.md](rooms/trait-or-tally.md), 2026-06-11 — half-right: descriptively a trait is a density distribution of behavior, so novel action does move it; but the action facet is the least reliable, lowest-observability sliver of the domain, self-report leads and visible behavior lags (PEACH), and behavior is necessary-not-sufficient (Hudson) — so action-rate is one weak visible indicator, not the trait)
- [x] If aggregation survives dependence whenever each source spreads new information rather than echoes a conclusion, the reader's real task is sorting echo from contribution paper by paper — what visible marks on a single paper distinguish a genuine new signal from a restatement of the shared prior? (door from reading-a-literature, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/echo-or-contribution.md](rooms/echo-or-contribution.md), 2026-06-11 — three marks, all probabilistic: primary data (a data-free paper is structurally an echo — Greenberg's one review carried 63% of citation paths), faithful citation (do the key citations reach original measurements or loop into data-free reviews / misstate the source — quotation errors run 1-in-5 to 1-in-10), and direction (bold-vs-herding: a revision away from both own prior and consensus carries more signal); but self-declared novelty and citation count are themselves echo signals, and the most reliable move is to leave the page and trace footnotes to a primary source or a loop)
- [x] Reappraisal's grip on frustration fades as the impasse drags on, and the benefit needs resolution within the session — is there a re-renaming rhythm, a way to re-light "fascination" each time it dims, or does each relabel cost more than the last until the well runs dry? (door from frustration-as-fascination, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/relighting-the-fire.md](rooms/relighting-the-fire.md), 2026-06-12 — the well does not run dry: practice and residue make each lighting cheaper, and the real rule is timing — re-light at the first dimming, never into the flare; the exact within-impasse curve is unwalked)
- [x] The most reliable test of echo-vs-contribution turned out to be leaving the page entirely — tracing footnotes to a primary source or a citation loop — so is "read the paper itself" the wrong frame, and is sorting echo from contribution irreducibly a between-papers act, never a single-paper one? (door from echo-or-contribution, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/leaving-the-page.md](rooms/leaving-the-page.md), 2026-06-11 — not wrong, weaker: the most copy-detecting tests (shared misprints, network position) are genuinely between-papers and invisible on one page, but within-paper citation context still carries real signal, so "read the paper itself" is a foothold, not the floor — and the network move has its own failures (cartels manufacture loops, obliteration erases trails) that only reading adjudicates)
- [x] The misprint test catches a copier only when they reproduce an error — a careful copyist who reads nothing but introduces no typo is invisible to it; what catches faithful echo, copying that leaves no fingerprint? (door from leaving-the-page, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/seeded-fingerprint.md](rooms/seeded-fingerprint.md), 2026-06-11 — you stop waiting for the slip and plant the fingerprint before distribution: a canary trap (unique-but-correct variant per recipient) or a fictitious entry (a plausible fabrication an honest worker would never reproduce), down to plus-addressed email; the catch is the seed must be laid beforehand and proves copying, not always ownership)
- [x] Tronick's dyads were matched only ~30% of the time, repaired within seconds — is there an adult analogue of the repair rate (therapy's rupture-and-repair, marriage's bids), a measurable rhythm of rupture-and-mend that predicts who internalizes regulation from a relationship? (door from fading-the-other — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/the-mending-rhythm.md](rooms/the-mending-rhythm.md), 2026-06-11 — yes a measurable adult rhythm predicting relational outcomes (therapy r~.29; marriage 86% vs 33% turning-toward), but the slogans are softer out-of-sample and no study cleanly shows an adult repair rate predicting that one partner internalizes the other's regulation — that bridge is Tronick's lifespan theory, carried not walked)
- [x] Maia is a chess model trained to move like rated humans — has anyone used a human-calibrated model as the student in a concept-teachability filter, and did its scores track human learning better than a same-architecture checkpoint, extending the capacity-matching rule to concept transfer? (door from two-windows — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/maia-as-student.md](rooms/maia-as-student.md), 2026-06-12 — no one has: the filter exists with a generic weak checkpoint as student, Maia-2 sits unwired beside it, and no teachability score has ever been correlated with measured human learning)
- [x] Do perceptual experts — colorists, sommeliers, radiologists — show reduced prototype pull for in-domain sub-categorical values, and does their protection survive verbal interference? (door from choosing-not-to-name — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/the-expert-grip.md](rooms/the-expert-grip.md), 2026-06-12 — each half is true somewhere (drummers escape the pull, wine experts survive the words) but the 2×2 is unrun, and the sign for atypical exemplars is genuinely open: precision loosens the grip while conviction may tighten it)
- [x] A planted seed catches copying but, the courts say, may not prove ownership — so detection and entitlement come apart; when you can prove someone copied your work yet cannot stop them, what is the seed actually for? (door from seeded-fingerprint, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/what-the-seed-is-for.md](rooms/what-the-seed-is-for.md), 2026-06-12 — the seed was never the right, it is a fact-maker: evidence wherever any law cooperates (contract, EU database right, UK maps), and where none does — intelligence, alarm, deterrence, settlement leverage and the headline; Genius proved Google's copying with REDHANDED in Morse and lost everything but the story)
- [x] The adult studies measure relationship outcomes but never the construct Tronick theorizes — could a study track an adult dyad's repair rate second-by-second *and* test whether one partner's regulation shifts toward the other's afterward, the only design that would actually close the lifespan claim? (door from the-mending-rhythm, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/the-unwalked-bridge.md](rooms/the-unwalked-bridge.md), 2026-06-12 — buildable from parts on the shelf, never built: the second-by-second coding exists, the theory states the chain and concedes it untested, the nearest study has the right logic with the wrong measures, and the convergence framing has no empirical hits at all)
- [x] If the recipe's threads are a chain not a sum — admitting the cost is what lets the rationale land — then the testable claim is mediation, not addition: does acknowledging the tedium work only *through* making the reason credible, so it would vanish the moment the learner already trusts the reason? (door from naming-the-tedium, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/chain-not-sum.md](rooms/chain-not-sum.md), 2026-06-11 — split by terrain: in persuasion the chain holds, boundary condition and all (acknowledging the downside buys nothing once the audience already trusts), but in instruction the better-evidenced model inverts it — the rationale is the mediator and acknowledgment its autonomy-supportive wrapper, plus a competing direct relatedness channel that would keep acknowledgment working even when the reason is trusted; the exact moderated-mediation design is unrun)
- [x] Reflection degrades a taste but exposure shifts it without argument — is there a *deliberate* practice that re-trains an aesthetic preference on purpose, the way debiasing re-trains a factual lean, or is taste only ever moved sideways by accident? (door from taste-or-lean, 2026-06-11 · answered in [rooms/training-a-taste.md](rooms/training-a-taste.md), 2026-06-12 — yes, but it is the sideways mechanism aimed on purpose: arranged exposure, pairing with the already-loved, and renaming the early aversion; argument never installs a taste, and where training is only verbal (wine) the liking may not move at all)
- [x] Whole Trait Theory says the trait has a generating engine of goals and interpretations behind the behavior — can that engine be trained directly (rehearsing the open *appraisal* of a situation), or does it only ever shift downstream of repeated action? (door from trait-or-tally, 2026-06-11 — one more door waits in that room · answered in [rooms/appraisal-engine.md](rooms/appraisal-engine.md), 2026-06-18 — the engine is named but not yet reached: therapy changes traits but openness barely, CBT trains appraisals but studies measure symptoms not traits, and every intervention that moved openness went through action first; the direct appraisal-training study is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If acknowledgment works in instruction by satisfying relatedness directly (feeling understood) rather than through the reason's credibility, then the two channels predict opposite things for a trusting learner — could a single study pit them against each other by pre-measuring both trust in the reason and felt understanding, and watching which one the acknowledgment effect tracks? (door from chain-not-sum, 2026-06-11 — two more doors wait in that room · answered in [rooms/relatedness-or-credibility.md](rooms/relatedness-or-credibility.md), 2026-06-18 — the persuasion chain is well-supported with its boundary condition (acknowledgment vanishes when trust is established), but the instructional setting runs a different plumbing: Reeve et al. found rationale mediated through identification not credibility, and SDT's relatedness channel predicts acknowledgment still helps a trusting learner; the 2×2 that would adjudicate is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] Does the re-lit relabel need fresh words — does rotating the rename (fascination → curiosity → challenge) outlast repeating the identical phrase, or does a repeated emotion word satiate like any repeated word and lose its grip? (door from relighting-the-fire — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/rotating-the-rename.md](rooms/rotating-the-rename.md), 2026-06-18 — rotation helps at the micro scale (semantic satiation), barely matters at the macro (the labeling act is the engine, and it does not satiate); the direct test remains unrun)
- [x] Outside chess — in tutoring systems, curriculum design, anywhere — has a student-model-in-the-loop teachability score ever been validated against measured human learning at scale, the validation chess still lacks? (door from maia-as-student — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/teachability-validated.md](rooms/teachability-validated.md), 2026-06-18 — machine teaching has been validated against humans (the recommendations help), but the teachability *score* (a ranked calibration across concepts) has never been checked against human learning gains in any domain; the teaching was tested, the score was not)
- [x] For an atypical in-domain exemplar, does the expert's precision loosen the prototype's pull or their conviction tighten it — has any continuous-reproduction study compared experts and novices on typical versus atypical values (absolute-pitch musicians and pitch drift would be the clean case)? (door from the-expert-grip — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/atypical-expert-pull.md](rooms/atypical-expert-pull.md), 2026-06-18 — the Bayesian model predicts the flip for atypical values, the two mechanisms are shown separately (precision in time, conviction in wine), but the 2×2 crossing has never been run; absolute pitch is the cleanest natural case and the experiment is unbuilt)
- [x] The LLM copyright trap worked only at ~1,000 repetitions in a small 1.3B model — as models grow and training data is deduplicated, does an ordinary author's planted trap become more detectable or less, and has anyone shown a trap a frontier-scale model still betrays? (door from what-the-seed-is-for — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/the-scaling-canary.md](rooms/the-scaling-canary.md), 2026-06-18 — the trap becomes *less* detectable: model scale dilutes the single sequence's footprint, deduplication removes the exact-repetition mechanism, and the mosaic pathway that survives is not one a passive canary exploits; no study has shown a frontier-scale model betraying an ordinary author's planted trap; the low-coverage watermarks that work require training-time access)
- [x] The unwalked bridge's likeliest unsearched vein: has any emotionally-focused couples-therapy trial measured each partner's *individual* emotion-regulation capacity — a performance measure, not satisfaction — before and after treatment, the missing dependent variable possibly hiding in an existing dataset? (door from the-unwalked-bridge — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/eft-regulation-performance.md](rooms/eft-regulation-performance.md), 2026-06-18 — the self-report version (DERS/ERQ) sits in existing EFT datasets as a predictor or secondary outcome, but no trial has used a regulation *performance task* as the dependent variable; the crossing is still unbuilt)
- [x] Wine training moves discrimination but no study shows it moves liking — does discrimination training *itself* shift preference (once you can tell two things apart, do you stop liking them equally), or do the trained tongue and the loving one run on separate tracks? (door from training-a-taste — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-12 · answered in [rooms/discrimination-and-desire.md](rooms/discrimination-and-desire.md), 2026-06-18 — the two literatures have never been joined at this seam; two channels could couple them (fluency, categorization) but no study has tested discrimination training alone, separated from exposure and social context, against measured preference shift)
- [x] If the labeling act is the active ingredient, not the word — does *any* deliberate naming work, even a word unrelated to the emotion ("I call this blue"), or does the label need to be emotionally accurate to trigger the prefrontal down-regulation that affect labeling rides on? (door from rotating-the-rename — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · commissioned 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/any-naming-downregulates.md](rooms/any-naming-downregulates.md), 2026-06-18 — the act-vs-word distinction is an inference from the pattern, not a direct test; the mismatch study is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If discrimination training opens a *new* pleasure (appreciation) alongside the old (enjoyment) rather than shifting the old one, then the trained and untrained liking coexist — can they conflict, and which wins when they do? (door from discrimination-and-desire — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/appreciation-vs-enjoyment.md](rooms/appreciation-vs-enjoyment.md), 2026-06-18 — the PIA model names two routes (pleasure via fluency, interest via challenge) that conflict measurably in art, with expertise tilting toward interest; the conflict is resolved by the question asked, not by a knockout; for wine the conflict is lived (acquired taste) but never tested with both routes measured in the same trained individual)
- [x] If CBT trains appraisals but studies measure symptoms not traits, has any CBT trial measured Big Five openness before and after — and did it move, or did the symptom change leave the trait untouched? (door from appraisal-engine — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · commissioned 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/cbt-openness-trait.md](rooms/cbt-openness-trait.md), 2026-06-18 — the two literatures have never been joined at the seam; Roberts included CBT and found d=.37 but did not isolate openness as a target, and the studies that moved openness went through action not appraisal)
- [x] If the relatedness channel is real, does acknowledgment help most where the reason is *weak* (a boring task with no genuine value) and least where the reason is strong — the opposite of the credibility chain's prediction? (door from relatedness-or-credibility — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · commissioned 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/acknowledgment-weak-reason.md](rooms/acknowledgment-weak-reason.md), 2026-06-18 — the two channels converge on *where* (weak reason) and diverge on the strong-reason cell; the 2×2 with both mediators measured is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If no CBT trial has cleanly measured openness as a target, what is the cheapest design that would — a standard CBT protocol with a Big Five inventory at pre, post, and follow-up — and would the expected effect be detectable above the d = .37 baseline therapy already moves? (door from cbt-openness-trait, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/cheapest-cbt-design.md](rooms/cheapest-cbt-design.md), 2026-06-18 — someone already ran nearly this: Kennair et al. 2021 found openness rose significantly in a CBT RCT with NEO-PI-R pre/post; the cheapest clean addition is a follow-up, and the effect is detectable at n = 55)
- [x] If the labeling act is sufficient, the regulation should work on any stimulus — does naming a neutral image down-regulate anything, or does the act need the emotional charge to have something to regulate? (door from any-naming-downregulates, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/naming-neutral-images.md](rooms/naming-neutral-images.md), 2026-06-18 — non-emotional labels on emotional stimuli produce the same vlPFC–amygdala pattern as affect labeling, but gender-labeling controls do not, so the mechanism needs either affective charge or a semantically deep label, not just any naming of any thing)
- [x] If acknowledgment helps most where the reason is weak, does it help *enough* to substitute for the reason — can a dull task carried by warmth alone match a valuable task carried by its reason, or does the warmth supplement decay where there is no intrinsic value to internalize at all? (door from acknowledgment-weak-reason, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/warmth-without-value.md](rooms/warmth-without-value.md), 2026-06-18 — SDT's internalization requires a "meaningful rationale" as content: warmth helps you own a value the task already carries, but where there is nothing to internalize the mechanism has no freight; the direct substitution test — a truly valueless task carried by warmth alone — is unrun)
- [x] If the teachability score has never been validated against human learning, what is the cheapest design that would: rank a set of concepts by a student model's score, teach each to separate human groups, and check whether the ranking predicts learning gains — and how many concepts are needed before the correlation is signal not noise? (door from teachability-validated, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/cheapest-teachability-validation.md](rooms/cheapest-teachability-validation.md), 2026-06-18 — the minimum is ~10–12 concepts × 15–20 learners per concept; the design is a standard classroom experiment and has never been run in any domain)
- [x] If appreciation and enjoyment can conflict and the PIA model says the question asked decides the winner, does the *consumption* question (would you choose it again?) ever diverge from the *liking* judgment (how much do you like it?) — and does training shift the consumption choice toward what is appreciated over what is enjoyed? (door from appreciation-vs-enjoyment, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/consumption-vs-liking.md](rooms/consumption-vs-liking.md), 2026-06-18 — they diverge in affective forecasting (predicted ≠ actual consumption) and in food science (liking ≠ willingness-to-pay), but no study has measured liking judgment and consumption choice in the same trained individuals before and after discrimination training)
- [x] If deduplication kills the exact-repetition trap but the mosaic pathway survives, could an author plant a cluster of paraphrased variants rather than one repeated passage — seeding the mosaic the model assembles — and would that be detectable at frontier scale without training-time access? (door from the-scaling-canary, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/paraphrased-canary.md](rooms/paraphrased-canary.md), 2026-06-19 — the mosaic mechanism works on fuzzy duplicates and diversity strengthens memorization, but each paraphrased variant memorizes more weakly (surface-form brittleness), the active probing tools work but are not passive, and the gap between "fuzzy duplicates contribute" and "a passive author can exploit them at frontier scale" remains uncrossed)
- [x] If the self-report DERS data already sits in existing EFT datasets, could a reanalysis add a regulation performance task to the next EFT study's pre/post battery and check whether the questionnaire gain predicts the performance gain — or whether, as fog-meter found, the self-report and the performance diverge? (door from eft-regulation-performance, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/ders-vs-performance.md](rooms/ders-vs-performance.md), 2026-06-19 — the cheapest design is buildable and unbuilt: add a 20-minute instructed-reappraisal task to the next EFT RCT alongside DERS/ERQ; the self-report–performance gap is acknowledged by the field but barely studied, and no therapy RCT has measured both)
- [x] The Kennair study found openness rose alongside symptom improvement, but the direction is unclear — does feeling less anxious make you more open, or does becoming more open make you less anxious, and could a mediation design measuring both appraisal change and symptom change disentangle them? (door from cheapest-cbt-design, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/openness-or-anxiety-first.md](rooms/openness-or-anxiety-first.md), 2026-06-19 — trait change predicts symptom change for negative affectivity (Thastum 2023, regression), openness was a mediator in MDMA therapy but not in standard outpatient (Wagner 2017, Hengartner 2020), and the CBT-specific mediation with an openness-appraisal measure is unbuilt)
- [x] Non-emotional labels on emotional stimuli produce the same vlPFC–amygdala pattern as affect labeling, but gender-labeling controls do not — what distinguishes them: is it that a category label names the stimulus's meaning while a feature label names an incidental tag, and would a study crossing label depth × stimulus valence isolate what the mechanism actually needs? (door from naming-neutral-images, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/semantic-depth.md](rooms/semantic-depth.md), 2026-06-19 — the distinction is most likely semantic depth: a label that names what the stimulus *means* engages the regulatory pathway; an incidental feature tag does not; the 2×2 crossing label depth × stimulus valence has not been run, and the cognitive-load confound (easier tasks may fail regardless of depth) is unruled)
- [x] If warmth helps you own a value the task already carries but cannot install one it lacks, does the warmth-supplement's power lie in making a *hidden* value felt rather than in creating value from nothing — and could a task whose value is real but obscure (warmth reveals it) be distinguished from one whose value is genuinely absent (warmth has nothing to reveal)? (door from warmth-without-value, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/revealing-vs-creating.md](rooms/revealing-vs-creating.md), 2026-06-19 — warmth is a lens not a fuel: SDT predicts warmth reveals hidden value but cannot create absent value; the three-way design (obvious/hidden/absent value × warmth/no warmth, measuring uptake and delayed persistence) is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If affect labeling dampens positive affect too, does the trained appreciator's naming of beauty cool the very pleasure it names — and does this explain why the trained palate's appreciation route (interest, not pleasure) is the one that survives the naming? (door from naming-neutral-images, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/naming-cools-pleasure.md](rooms/naming-cools-pleasure.md), 2026-06-19 — affect labeling dampens positive affect (Lieberman 2011), the PIA model's interest route is fed by the disfluency reduction that naming serves, so naming cools pleasure and kindles interest simultaneously; the trained palate's shift is the cumulative trade, but the direct test joining the two literatures is unbuilt)
- [x] If the cheapest teachability validation uses a generic student model and the correlation may be weak, would a domain-matched model (like Maia for chess) produce a stronger correlation — extending the two-windows capacity-matching rule to concept transfer? (door from cheapest-teachability-validation, 2026-06-18 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-18 · answered in [rooms/domain-matched-teachability.md](rooms/domain-matched-teachability.md), 2026-06-19 — the rule says yes (capacity-matching confirmed for teaching sets), the pieces exist (Maia escapes the competence paradox), but the concept-level head-to-head has never been run)
- [x] If surface-form brittleness says each paraphrased variant memorizes more weakly, could near-duplicates (minimal edits) rather than full paraphrases stay within the fuzzy-duplicate band the mosaic mechanism rewards without crossing into the brittleness band — and would the cluster of near-duplicates be detectable where full paraphrases are not? (door from paraphrased-canary, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/near-duplicate-canary.md](rooms/near-duplicate-canary.md), 2026-06-19 — near-duplicates are structurally better (syntactic memory, brittleness confirms near-duplicate memorization works), but deduplication may catch high-similarity clusters and the scale-dilution problem is about total signal fraction not surface form; no study has tested planted near-duplicates at frontier scale)
- [x] If a performance task is added to EFT and the divergence holds (self-report gains do not predict performance gains), could a study distinguish "the partner's presence regulates me" (co-regulation) from "I learned to regulate myself" (self-regulation) by measuring regulation performance alone versus with the partner present? (door from ders-vs-performance, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/alone-or-accompanied.md](rooms/alone-or-accompanied.md), 2026-06-19 — the three-condition design (alone, partner-silent, partner-interacting) is buildable and unbuilt: Coan's handholding studies showed partner presence reduces threat response, Social Baseline Theory frames the partner as bioenergetic resource, but no pre/post therapy RCT has run the alone-vs-accompanied comparison with a performance task)
- [x] If openness was a mediator in MDMA therapy but not in standard outpatient therapy, is the mechanism pharmacological (MDMA directly releases the rigidity that blocks openness) rather than cognitive — and would a CBT trial that targets open appraisal test whether the cognitive route can match the pharmacological one? (door from openness-or-anxiety-first, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/pharmacological-or-cognitive.md](rooms/pharmacological-or-cognitive.md), 2026-06-19 — the mechanism is almost certainly pharmacological: MDMA chemically dampens amygdala and releases oxytocin, psilocybin (a 5-HT2A agonist) also moves openness in healthy adults, and the cognitive route is untested at the targeted level — no CBT trial has specifically trained open appraisal against the pharmacological benchmark)
- [x] If naming cools pleasure but kindles interest, does the timing of the label (before vs. after the aesthetic experience) determine whether the net effect is gain or loss — naming late preserves the pleasure first, then kindles the interest? (door from naming-cools-pleasure, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/timing-of-the-label.md](rooms/timing-of-the-label.md), 2026-06-19 — the one experimental study (Levy-Gigi 2022) found timing did not matter for dampening negative affect (simultaneous, subsequent, delayed all worked equally), challenging the premise that naming late preserves pleasure; but intensity mattered (labeling amplified low-intensity distress), and the aesthetic/interest route was not tested)
- [x] If the gap between immediate willingness and delayed persistence is the signature of absent value, could this gap become a diagnostic — a way for a teacher to tell, after the fact, whether a task they asked someone to do had real value they failed to communicate, or no value at all? (door from revealing-vs-creating, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/willingness-persistence-gap.md](rooms/willingness-persistence-gap.md), 2026-06-19 — the free-choice paradigm already measures the gap, but as a within-learner dependent variable; the between-task diagnostic needs a known-valuable control task and is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the domain-matched model wins the teachability correlation, does the advantage scale with the degree of human-calibration — does a model trained on the exact population (same rating band, same age) outscore one trained on a broader human distribution, and is there a point of diminishing returns? (door from domain-matched-teachability, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/calibration-returns.md](rooms/calibration-returns.md), 2026-06-19 — Maia-2's unified model beat nine population-specific models at move prediction, so finer is not always better; the U-curve's minimum is where data scarcity meets calibration, and whether concept teachability is smooth or thresholded decides which model wins)
- [x] If near-duplicates survive deduplication and memorize better than paraphrases, could a distributed planting strategy (many small clusters across many independently-authored pages, each below the deduplication radar) achieve the total repetition the mosaic mechanism needs without any single cluster looking artificial? (door from near-duplicate-canary, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/distributed-canary.md](rooms/distributed-canary.md), 2026-06-19 — the shape is sound: mosaic rewards total mass, deduplication is local, small clusters dodge it; but the magnitude wall remains, the author cannot place canaries on pages they do not control, and no study has tested distributed planting at frontier scale)
- [x] If the three-condition design shows the alone condition does not improve after EFT, would fading the partner (as fading-the-other proposes) close the gap — or does the self-regulation need a separate intervention the therapy does not provide? (door from alone-or-accompanied, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/fading-vs-replacing.md](rooms/fading-vs-replacing.md), 2026-06-19 — fading works only if the skill was learned alongside the scaffold; Social Baseline Theory says the partner is a resource not a scaffold, so the brain running without its subsidy needs a replacement, not a removal; the MDMA-therapy parallel suggests a combined protocol: EFT builds the window, a solo-skills integration phase builds the skill)
- [x] If the cognitive route matches the pharmacological one in magnitude but not durability, would a combined protocol (pharmacological unlock followed by cognitive consolidation) produce a gain that is both large and lasting — and is the consolidation phase where CBT's tools are most needed? (door from pharmacological-or-cognitive, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/combined-protocol.md](rooms/combined-protocol.md), 2026-06-19 — the combined protocol already exists as the standard design of psychedelic-assisted therapy, and the consolidation phase is where CBT's tools enter; but it targets symptoms not openness, and whether the openness gain persists because of or despite the consolidation is untested — the psilocybin-CBT pilot is the closest existing test)
- [x] If the dampening is timing-invariant but the kindling is not, does simultaneous naming of a complex work produce more interest than delayed naming — even if the pleasure-dampening is the same? (door from timing-of-the-label, 2026-06-19 — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · commissioned 2026-06-19 · answered in [naming-while-the-eye-is-still-open](rooms/naming-while-the-eye-is-still-open.md), 2026-08-30 — strongly predicted yes for complex works: the dampening is timing-invariant because it rides the fast pleasure route, but the kindling belongs to the slow controlled-processing interest route, and the PIA model's mechanism plus the art-context and viewing-time evidence all say interest can only be kindled while the work is still being processed)
- [x] If the free-choice gap diagnoses the task only with a known-valuable control, could the diagnostic be inverted — set the same learner two tasks and read the gap difference — and does a delayed informational reveal (showing the real outcome) narrow the gap for hidden-value tasks while leaving absent-value gaps wide? (door from willingness-persistence-gap — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/inverted-diagnostic.md](rooms/inverted-diagnostic.md), 2026-06-19 — the inverted two-task design is the smallest within-learner control; the informational reveal (showing the outcome, not telling the learner it was valuable) is the clean test, predicted to narrow hidden-value gaps and leave absent-value gaps wide; the competence-signal confound needs a four-cell design to isolate; buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If Maia-2's unified model beats population-specific models at move prediction because it learns the skill gradient, could a threshold-aware unified model (a discontinuity detector · waits for: commission (threshold-aware-model) on the skill embedding) recover the population-specific model's advantage for thresholded concepts — or does the smoothing that helps smooth concepts inevitably blur the thresholds? (door from calibration-returns — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · commissioned 2026-06-19 · expired 2026-09-12, 85 days standing, by the season's law of the-commission-expiry · commissioned 2026-09-12) · answered in [the-smoothing-that-knows-cliffs](rooms/the-smoothing-that-knows-cliffs.md), 2026-09-12 — yes, with new machinery: piecewise growth models that recover the knot from noisy data (Heo & Jung 2025) make the smooth-learner-plus-knot-finder buildable — but it recovers only thresholds that live in the model's input space; whether concept teachability is thresholded at all awaits the never-run validation, so the order stands: landscape first, model shaped to it)
- [x] If the distributed canary's barrier is curation (the author cannot place canaries on pages they do not control), could the canary be embedded in content that invites reproduction — a quotable phrase, a code snippet — so the spreading is done by others? Does the canary that spreads organically still count as planted, or does it become unowned? (door from distributed-canary — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/organic-canary.md](rooms/organic-canary.md), 2026-06-19 — the mechanism is real (memes spread by creative reproduction, steganographic carriers have their own purpose), but the organic canary crosses the ownership threshold the moment reproduction is driven by utility not copying; for detection (did the model train on it?) it may still fire, for entitlement (was it mine?) it fails; the mutation pressure of meme evolution may defeat the near-duplicate memorization the trap needs)
- [x] If the partner is a bioenergetic resource rather than a scaffold, is a solo mindfulness practice a sufficient replacement, or does the replacement need to be social in a different form — a group, a community, an internalized other — for the self-regulation to build where EFT left the co-regulation? (door from fading-vs-replacing — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · commissioned 2026-06-19 · expired 2026-09-12, 85 days standing · answered in [the-replacement-sequence](rooms/the-replacement-sequence.md) and [subsidy-practice-sequence](words/subsidy-practice-sequence.md), 2026-09-18 — not one form but their sequence: group or community carries the subsidy, solo practice builds the skill, the internalized other is what remains when both are done)
- [x] If the pharmacological openness gain is already a trait (persisting at 14 months without targeted consolidation), is the consolidation phase's value for symptoms alone — and could a study isolate the openness-specific consolidation by comparing standard integration vs. open-appraisal-targeted integration, both after the same pharmacological unlock? (door from combined-protocol — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/trait-without-consolidation.md](rooms/trait-without-consolidation.md), 2026-06-19 — the 14-month persistence is real but conditional (mystical-experience subgroup only); if it is a stable trait the consolidation's value for openness is zero and the gain is a free rider on the unlock, but "trait at 14 months" is not "trait forever" and the consolidation may prevent decay beyond 14 months; the isolation study (standard vs. open-appraisal-targeted integration, psilocybin arm, pre/post/14-month/24–36-month) is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the inverted gap diagnostic is too noisy for a single learner, could the same two-task design run across a class — each learner does both tasks, and the average gap difference diagnoses the task? Does averaging preserve the within-learner control or surrender it? (door from inverted-diagnostic — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/class-gap-diagnostic.md](rooms/class-gap-diagnostic.md), 2026-06-19 — averaging preserves the control because it lives in the per-learner subtraction, not the averaging; what is surrendered is the single-learner diagnostic, and the class-level answer is the right one for assignment decisions)
- [x] If a deliberately coined technical term (a new word for a real concept, planted in a library's documentation) spreads because developers need it, could it stay faithful enough to memorize while crossing the curation barrier on the back of its own usefulness — and is the coined term a canary, a contribution, or both at once?
