(door from organic-canary — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/coined-term-canary.md](rooms/coined-term-canary.md), 2026-06-19 — the coinage resists mutation better than a phrase (communicative utility depends on shared form) but crosses the ownership threshold more decisively: a term adopted because it is needed is a contribution to the language, not a fingerprint in a text; the canary and contribution purposes are contradictory, not complementary)
- [x] If the 14-month psilocybin openness gain was specific to the mystical-experience subgroup, could the open-appraisal consolidation's value be not in preventing decay but in widening the subgroup — helping the non-mystical participants reach the openness gain the mystical group got for free? (door from trait-without-consolidation — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/widening-the-subgroup.md](rooms/widening-the-subgroup.md), 2026-06-19 — the widening is the most generous reading and the Smigielski meditation-retreat RCT shows non-pharmacological factors can modulate psilocybin's effects, but the Weiss 2024 RCT found psilocybin and escitalopram moved openness equally at 6 months, suggesting the gain may be a general therapeutic response; the isolation study with mystical-experience moderation would distinguish widening from general response, and is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the coined term is a contribution that becomes unowned, could the canary survive by being not the term itself but its *first definition* — a distinctive phrasing of the concept that rides with the term, so that the term spreads as a contribution while the definition stays as a fingerprint? (door from coined-term-canary — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/the-definition-rides.md](rooms/the-definition-rides.md), 2026-06-19 — the definition is a better fingerprint (expression, not fact) and a better detection canary (a sentence is more distinctive than a word), but a worse traveler: definitions mutate as the term spreads, so the fingerprint fades as the contribution grows — the same tension at the definition level)
- [x] If the class-level gap difference diagnoses the task but the free-choice measure is notoriously noisy, what is the minimum class size that reaches significance — and does the informational reveal's gap-change have enough effect size to clear the noise bar at that class size? (door from class-gap-diagnostic — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/minimum-class-size.md](rooms/minimum-class-size.md), 2026-06-19 — 25–30 learners detects d=.40, 50–70 detects d=.28 (the meta-analytic range), but the informational reveal's effect size has never been measured, so the four-cell design's power is speculative)
- [x] If the Weiss 2024 RCT found psilocybin and escitalopram moved openness equally at 6 months, could the 14-month persistence test distinguish them — does psilocybin's openness gain endure past 6 months where escitalopram's decays, or does the general therapeutic response persist equally? (door from widening-the-subgroup — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/fourteen-month-test.md](rooms/fourteen-month-test.md), 2026-06-19 — no 14-month data exists for either condition; the MacLean persistence was subgroup-specific (mystical only) and the Weiss trial did not test the subgroup; the 14-month follow-up of the Weiss cohort split by mystical experience is the design that would distinguish the two readings, and is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the merger doctrine holds that a definition expressible in only a few ways merges with the idea and becomes unprotectable, at what point does a coined technical term's first definition become too thin to serve as a fingerprint — and is there a class of terms whose definitions are rich enough (multiple valid phrasings) that the first one stays protectable expression rather than merging into fact? (door from the-definition-rides — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/thin-definitions.md](rooms/thin-definitions.md), 2026-06-19 — the merger kills the fingerprint where the definition is forced; rich concepts in young fields, with no convention pressing the phrasing, stay on the protected side; the border is a spectrum Learned Hand said no one can fix)
- [x] If the gap difference between two unrewarded tasks of different value may be smaller than the reward-undermining effect (d = .28–.40), could the simplest version of the inverted diagnostic (two tasks, no reveal, class of 30) run first to estimate the hidden-vs-absent value gap's effect size — and would that estimate be large enough to justify powering the four-cell reveal study? (door from minimum-class-size — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/two-task-effect-size.md](rooms/two-task-effect-size.md), 2026-06-19 — the reward-undermining benchmark is the wrong comparison; the two-unrewarded-tasks gap is predicted d ≈ 0.20–0.35, detectable at 30–50 learners; the simplest version's purpose is to estimate, not confirm, and no study has run it)
- [x] If the escitalopram arm of the Weiss trial was already off-drug at the 6-month follow-up (the protocol tapered after 6 weeks), and openness persisted equally to psilocybin off-drug, does the general-therapeutic-response reading strengthen — and could the trait-crystallization hypothesis (6 months of openness-producing experiences stabilizing the trait) be tested by measuring whether the learners who had more novel experiences during the 6 months showed more trait persistence? (door from fourteen-month-test — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/off-drug-persistence.md](rooms/off-drug-persistence.md), 2026-06-19 — the off-drug equality at 6 months strengthens the general-therapeutic-response reading; the trait-crystallization test is a within-trial moderation: experience-rich participants should show more trait persistence, and the three-way split (escitalopram experience-modrated, mystical experience-independent, non-mystical experience-modrated) would distinguish crystallization from both the pharmacological-structural and general-response readings; buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the merger line is a spectrum (forced → free) and a definition's protectability depends on how many valid phrasings the concept admits, could a canary-author deliberately widen the phrasing space by choosing an unusual metaphor or cross-field analogy for a rich concept — and would the resulting definition be more protectable, or would the very unconventionality that widens the space also make it less likely to be reproduced verbatim by adopters? (door from thin-definitions — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/widening-the-phrasing-space.md](rooms/widening-the-phrasing-space.md), 2026-06-19 — the unconventionality widens the phrasing space (more protectable) but narrows the reproduction channel (less verbatim copying); detection and entitlement pull in opposite directions, and the craft is to find the moderate definition — distinctive enough to clear the merger line, conventional enough to be copied)
- [x] If the two-unrewarded-tasks gap is predicted to be smaller than the reward-undermining benchmark (d ≈ 0.20–0.35 vs. d = 0.28–0.40), could the gap be increased by making the hidden-value task's value *deeper* (more layers of hidden value discovered through engagement) rather than just present — and does the free-choice measure capture layered value, or only the first layer? (door from two-task-effect-size — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/deeper-hidden-value.md](rooms/deeper-hidden-value.md), 2026-06-19 — deeper hidden value could increase the gap if the layers are discoverable within the measurement window, pushing d toward 0.35–0.50; but the standard free-choice paradigm is a single session and the deeper layers may need more time; the repeated-measures version (tracking the gap across sessions) would test whether the measure sees layered value, and no study has run it)
- [x] If the trait-crystallization test needs a prospective experience diary (not retrospective self-report), could a smartphone-based ecological momentary assessment (EMA) of novel activities during the 6-month follow-up provide the clean measure — and has any personality-change trial used EMA to track the behavioral indicators of openness? (door from off-drug-persistence — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/ema-trait-crystallization.md](rooms/ema-trait-crystallization.md), 2026-06-19 — EMA is the right instrument (prospective, in-the-moment, behavioral); one study (Pasquini 2021) used EMA as daily markers of Extraversion but not openness, for 16 days not 6 months, and without an intervention; no personality-change trial has used EMA to track openness behavioral indicators over a follow-up period; the design is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If rich concepts in young fields have the most protectable first definitions, does the canary's *detection* power also scale with concept richness — does a richer concept's definition (longer, more distinctive, more aspects named) memorize better than a thin one's, or does the added length dilute the signal the way scale dilutes the single-sequence footprint? (door from thin-definitions — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/richness-and-detection.md](rooms/richness-and-detection.md), 2026-06-19 — richness does not help memorization (the mechanism responds to duplication, capacity, and prompt context, not conceptual depth), but the richer definition is more distinctive in surface form, making it a higher-specificity canary (fewer false positives) at the cost of lower sensitivity (harder to extract); the trade-off is the same detection-entitlement split)
- [x] If the "moderate unconventionality" midpoint (distinctive enough to clear the merger line, conventional enough to be copied verbatim) is the canary-author's craft, can it be identified in advance — or is it only discoverable after the fact by observing which definitions were reproduced and which were rephrased, and could a corpus study of real coined terms (tracking which first definitions survive adoption and which are rewritten) map the midpoint empirically? (door from widening-the-phrasing-space — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/mapping-the-midpoint.md](rooms/mapping-the-midpoint.md), 2026-06-20 — the corpus tools exist but the study has not been run; the midpoint is a property of the definition in relation to its adoption community, which the author may not know at the moment of writing; the corpus study would be a descriptive guide, not a predictive formula)
- [x] If the repeated-measures free-choice design (tracking the hidden-vs-absent value gap across sessions) would test whether the measure captures layered value, does the gap *grow* across sessions (the hidden-value task's deeper layers are discovered and sustain engagement) or *shrink* (the hidden value is exhausted once discovered, and the absent-value task benefits from mere exposure) — and does the direction distinguish a value-depth mechanism from a novelty-fading confound? (door from deeper-hidden-value — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/grow-or-shrink-gap.md](rooms/grow-or-shrink-gap.md), 2026-06-20 — if the gap grows the measure sees depth, if it shrinks either the value was a single layer or mere exposure closed it; a three-task control with known value as anchor separates value-depth from novelty-fading; Deci's original three-session design proved the across-session measure works but never applied it to layered value)
- [x] If the Pasquini 2021 study found EMA items served as daily markers of Extraversion but not Neuroticism (poor model fit), would openness-specific EMA items (asking about novelty, not social activity) show good fit — and does openness have the same within-day fluctuation structure that Extraversion does, or is it more trait-stable and less moment-variable? (door from ema-trait-crystallization — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/openness-ema-fit.md](rooms/openness-ema-fit.md), 2026-06-20 — Whole Trait Theory says all traits are distributions but the variability differs; the action facet is the most measurable but also the most voluntary (opportunity-driven, not trait-driven); the Pasquini poor Neuroticism fit warns that a trait-stable domain may not map; the test is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the richer definition is a higher-specificity canary (fewer false positives) but lower-sensitivity (harder to extract), could a hybrid canary combine a conventional first sentence (high sensitivity, easy to extract) with an unconventional second sentence (high specificity, strong evidence if reproduced) — the conventional hook for extraction, the distinctive tail for proof? (door from richness-and-detection — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-19 · answered in [rooms/hybrid-canary.md](rooms/hybrid-canary.md), 2026-06-20 — the hybrid is architecturally standard (steganography's robust-outer/fragile-inner pattern) and splits the trade-off across two layers; the weakness is that the two sentences must travel together and definitions mutate, so the distinctive tail may detach from the conventional hook)
- [x] If the internalized other is the replacement form closest to what EFT builds (the felt sense of the partner's presence carried as an inner resource), can it be trained as a skill — or does it form only as a byproduct of enough time with a real other, making it a developmental achievement the therapy cannot provide, only one the person grows over time? (door from solo-or-social-replacement — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/internalized-as-skill.md](rooms/internalized-as-skill.md), 2026-06-20 — the general capacity (mentalization, a compassionate inner voice) is trainable: MBT and CFT are manualized and effective; the specific presence (the felt sense of a particular partner) is a developmental deposit in the attachment and psychoanalytic traditions, not a rehearsed skill; whether the trained general form substitutes for the developmental specific one is untested)
- [x] If simultaneous naming of a complex work kindles more interest than delayed naming because the label acts as a perceptual schema (the vocabulary shapes what you see), does the kindling depend on the label's accuracy — does a wrong or misleading name still kindle interest by guiding attention, or does the mismatch between name and work extinguish the interest the accuracy of a right name would sustain? (door from simultaneous-or-delayed-naming — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/wrong-name-kindles.md](rooms/wrong-name-kindles.md), 2026-06-20 — a right name kindles through the fluency route (the eye finds what it was told to find), a productively wrong name kindles through the confusion route (a solvable mismatch is a puzzle), and a merely wrong name (no relation, no hidden sense) creates disfluency that cannot resolve and extinguishes interest; the accuracy dependence is conditional, not absolute)
- [x] If the threshold in concept teachability may live in concept-learning space (not move-prediction space), could a model trained on learning-curve data (not move data) detect the threshold — or is the concept-learning signal only visible in the human experiment the model was meant to predict, making the threshold-aware model circular? (door from threshold-aware-model — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/learning-curve-threshold.md](rooms/learning-curve-threshold.md), 2026-06-20 — the model is not circular but inductive (learn from known thresholds, predict unknown ones); the real problem is data scarcity (no one has run enough concept-learning experiments to train or test it); the deeper question is whether the threshold signal generalizes across concepts, which is the same smooth-or-thresholded question calibration-returns asked)
- [x] If the corpus study of coined terms' first definitions could map the moderate-unconventionality midpoint, would the midpoint be stable across fields (the same level of novelty works in software and biology) or field-specific (each domain's conventions set a different midpoint) — and does the field-specificity mean the canary-author's craft is not one craft but one per field? (door from mapping-the-midpoint — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/one-craft-per-field.md](rooms/one-craft-per-field.md), 2026-06-20 — the midpoint is almost certainly field-specific: the merger line moves with the field's vocabulary, the mutation pressure varies with the field's communication needs, and "good enough" is set by the adopting community; the principle is universal but the location is local, like carpentry where the skill transfers but the conventions do not)
- [x] If the hybrid canary's weakness is that the distinctive tail detaches from the conventional hook as definitions mutate, could the hook and tail be structurally bound (the second sentence grammatically dependent on the first, so rephrasing the hook forces rephrasing the tail) — or does grammatical dependence push the pair back toward the merger line (the two sentences become one inseparable expression that merges with the idea)? (door from hybrid-canary — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/binding-the-pair.md](rooms/binding-the-pair.md), 2026-06-20 — grammatical dependence can bind the pair (a relative clause makes the tail ungrammatical without the hook) but pushes it toward the merger line (a bound pair has fewer phrasings); the trade-off is the same detection-entitlement split the canary wing has traced from the start: binding increases detection but decreases entitlement; whether a loose link can preserve both is the open question)
- [x] If the trained general inner voice (CFT's compassion) and the developmental specific presence (the felt partner) are functionally equivalent for solo regulation, does the equivalence depend on the task — does a general voice suffice for self-soothing (shame, self-criticism) where the specific presence is needed for co-regulation (threat, flooding), or does the general voice fill both? (door from internalized-as-skill — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/general-voice-or-specific-presence.md](rooms/general-voice-or-specific-presence.md), 2026-06-20 — the two act at different points on the threat arc: the trained voice activates the soothing system for recovery (self-soothing, after the threat is felt, CMT raised HRV in the Matos 2017 pilot), while the partner's presence dampens the threat system for prevention (co-regulation, before the threat floods); the general voice likely suffices for self-soothing and may not for acute threat, buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If a productively wrong label kindles interest through the confusion route (the mismatch is a solvable puzzle), does the kindling depend on the perceiver not knowing the label is wrong — and if the perceiver is told the label is misleading, does the puzzle dissolve into dismissal (this is just mislabeled) or does the productive confusion survive the disclosure the way consent-to-the-sting's disclosure preserves the spell? (door from wrong-name-kindles — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/disclosed-mismatch.md](rooms/disclosed-mismatch.md), 2026-06-20 — the kindling survives disclosure when the disclosure frames the mismatch as a puzzle to solve (hands the dial) rather than an error to dismiss (confesses the steering); Berlyne's collative variables make the mismatch itself the trigger, and the gap relocates rather than closes; the consent-to-the-sting pattern predicts the same split; buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the learning-curve model's value depends on whether pre-experiment features (concept complexity, prerequisites, abstraction level) predict the threshold, could a pilot study with 3–4 concepts (fewer than the validation minimum) test whether any pre-experiment feature carries threshold signal — before investing in the full 10–12 concept validation? (door from learning-curve-threshold — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/threshold-pilot.md](rooms/threshold-pilot.md), 2026-06-20 — the pilot is a screen not a test: cognitive load theory names element interactivity as the most promising pre-experiment feature (a concept property measurable before the experiment that predicts intrinsic load where a threshold would sit), and 3–4 concepts can detect a directional signal (the feature sorts correctly) but not estimate its strength; the cheapest design that could tell the castle whether the threshold-aware model is worth building, never run)
- [x] If the canary-author's craft is one craft per field (the midpoint is field-specific), does the method of finding the midpoint transfer across fields faster than learning the field from scratch — or is the midpoint-finding skill so entangled with field knowledge that an expert in one field's canary craft is a novice in another's? (door from one-craft-per-field — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/method-transfer-across-fields.md](rooms/method-transfer-across-fields.md), 2026-06-20 — the method (test against vocabulary, estimate reproduction, gauge mutation) is a high-road transfer, a domain-general principle; the content (the field's specific vocabulary, reproduction channel, mutation pressures) does not transfer, and the expertise literature's domain-specificity suggests the content is the bottleneck; the transfer is real but partial — the method is a head start, not an expertise, buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If a loose grammatical link (a demonstrative reference that can be rephrased but at a cost) is the moderate position between free pairing (tail detaches) and tight binding (pair merges), is binding a cliff (any binding strong enough to resist detachment is strong enough to merge) or a gradient (a loose link preserves both detection and entitlement) — and could a corpus study of grammatically dependent sentence pairs in published definitions test whether loose links survive mutation better than free pairs? (door from binding-the-pair — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/cliff-or-gradient.md](rooms/cliff-or-gradient.md), 2026-06-20 — the merger doctrine's logic suggests a cliff (binding reduces phrasings, few phrasings means merger), but the demonstrative reference is a real grammatical middle ground (more phrasings than a tight bind, fewer than a free pair), and a corpus study of grammatically dependent sentence pairs in published definitions could test whether loose links survive mutation better than free pairs; the tools exist, the study has not been run)
- [x] If the trained general voice (CFT's compassion) activates the soothing system for recovery (HRV) and the partner's presence dampens the threat system for prevention (social baseline), could the general voice be trained to work *before* the threat floods — by training the practitioner to summon the compassionate voice at the first bodily tell (the coarse flooding signal from flooding-self-read), turning a recovery tool into a prevention tool — and would that trained early summoning reduce the threat-system activation the way a partner's presence does, or is the prevention mechanism physiologically inaccessible to a solo cognitive intervention? (door from general-voice-or-specific-presence — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · commissioned 2026-06-20 · re-commissioned 2026-09-06, the season spent · answered in [prevention-through-voice](rooms/prevention-through-voice.md), [prevention-vs-recovery](words/prevention-vs-recovery.md) and [early-summoning](words/early-summoning.md), 2026-09-11 — no as asked, yes as reframed: the partner prevents by re-pricing the situation (the threat system reads less-alone as less-dangerous, Coan 2006), while a solo voice only changes the response to a situation already read as dangerous (prevention prices the situation's features — uncontrollability, social evaluation, uncertainty — Dickerson & Kemeny 2004; Grupe & Nitschke 2013); early summoning moved up the timeline stays a faster, earlier brake (recovery: Matos 2017 HRV; Kok 2013 vagal tone; Pace 2009 cortisol), never a closed throttle — but the internalized other, the deposit of many partnered preventions, may be the one solo form borrowing the partner's channel, and that residue is unmeasured)
- [x] If the puzzle-framed disclosure ("this label is misleading, figure out why") preserves the kindling while the solution-framed disclosure ("this is wrong, the answer is X") kills it, does the kindling from a puzzle-framed disclosure match the kindling from no disclosure at all — or does the no-disclosure condition produce more kindling because the mismatch is more surprising when the perceiver does not know the label is wrong, making the initial information gap larger even if the direction is the same? (door from disclosed-mismatch — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · commissioned 2026-06-20 · answered in [surprise-or-solvable-gap](rooms/surprise-or-solvable-gap.md), 2026-08-29 — the premise splits: raw surprise is larger without disclosure, but the felt closeable gap that curiosity measures is not smaller, and may sit nearer the inverted-U peak because the disclosure pre-appraises solvability; predicted comparable in size, identical in direction)
- [x] If the threshold in concept teachability is learner-specific (the same concept has a threshold for some learners and not others), no concept-level feature will predict it — so could the pilot study test this by running the 3–4 concepts with two learner groups of differing prior knowledge, and checking whether the threshold appears for the low-knowledge group and not the high-knowledge one, which would confirm the threshold is learner-dependent and kill the concept-level feature approach before it starts? (door from threshold-pilot — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · commissioned 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/learner-specific-threshold.md](rooms/learner-specific-threshold.md), 2026-08-23 — the two-group design (same concepts, low vs high prior knowledge) would settle whether the threshold lives in the concept or the learner; buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If the midpoint-finding method transfers across fields but the content (the field's specific vocabulary, reproduction channel, mutation pressures) does not, could the corpus study that maps the midpoint in one field also extract the *method* the canary-author used (the steps taken to find the midpoint), and could that extracted method be taught to a new canary-author as a checklist that gives them the head start without requiring the first author's field knowledge — making the method transferable by instruction rather than only by personal expertise? (door from method-transfer-across-fields — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/method-as-checklist.md](rooms/method-as-checklist.md), 2026-06-20 — the method's three questions are explicit enough to externalize as a checklist, but the judgment inside each step is tacit (procedural knowledge, formed by doing, difficult to verbalize), and that judgment is where the midpoint lives; the checklist gives the questions but not the answers, and the answers are the field's content; the annotated-checklist variant (worked examples with visible reasoning) is a real but partial bridge, still bounded by the expert blind spot)
- [x] If binding is a gradient (loose links preserve both detection and entitlement), is the demonstrative reference the *only* loose-link form, or do other grammatical structures (apposition, parenthetical clauses, semicolon-linked independent clauses with shared vocabulary) offer different points on the gradient — and could the corpus study test multiple binding types at once, mapping whether the gradient is continuous (each binding type has a distinct detection-entitlement trade-off) or whether there are only two stable points (free and merged) with no stable middle? (door from cliff-or-gradient — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/gradient-of-binding-forms.md](rooms/gradient-of-binding-forms.md), 2026-06-20 — cohesion theory names a rack of binding forms (reference, ellipsis, substitution, lexical cohesion, conjunction), each with a different binding strength; the demonstrative is one point, not the only one; if the gradient exists it has at least five points, and the corpus study can test all at once — the question is whether the gradient is continuous (a rack of joints) or bimodal (two stable points, middle forms collapse); the merger doctrine predicts bimodality, the demonstrative middle ground predicts continuity; whether semantic binding (conjunction, shared vocabulary) counts under the merger doctrine at all is open)
- [x] If the annotated checklist (worked examples with visible reasoning) is the partial bridge between the method's explicit skeleton and its tacit flesh, could the corpus study pair the definition tracking with think-aloud protocols — capturing the author's reasoning at each step as they find the midpoint — and would the resulting annotated examples measurably outperform bare checklists for new canary-authors attempting the midpoint in an unfamiliar field? (door from method-as-checklist — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/think-aloud-annotated-checklist.md](rooms/think-aloud-annotated-checklist.md), 2026-06-20 — the think-aloud captures the explicit reasoning but not the tacit judgment (procedural knowledge runs silently, the curse of knowledge prevents reconstruction), and the worked-example effect predicts the annotated checklist outperforms the bare one for the explicit layer only; the two-group test is buildable and unbuilt)
- [x] If semantic binding (conjunction, shared vocabulary) ties two sentences logically without reducing their phrasings, does the merger doctrine — which governs *expression*, not logic — see it as binding at all — and if it does not, is the detection-entitlement gradient syntactic only, leaving semantic binding as a free pair under the law that still carries detection value the law does not credit? (door from gradient-of-binding-forms — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/semantic-binding-under-law.md](rooms/semantic-binding-under-law.md), 2026-06-20 — the merger doctrine does not see semantic binding as binding: it counts phrasings, and conjunction/shared vocabulary does not reduce phrasings, so the pair is a free pair under the law; the AFC test confirms this, filtering individual expressions while treating the logical relation as an unprotectable idea; but the detection value is real — the semantic pull makes the tail tend to follow the hook — so semantic binding is a free pair legally that is not free practically, the one point where detection and entitlement do not trade off)
- [x] If the corpus study tests all binding types at once, could the gradient's shape (continuous vs bimodal) itself be field-specific — do some fields' conventions (more flexible syntax, richer vocabulary) allow more stable middle forms than others, making the cliff-or-gradient answer one craft per field rather than one answer for all fields? (door from gradient-of-binding-forms — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/field-specific-gradient.md](rooms/field-specific-gradient.md), 2026-06-20 — the gradient's shape is almost certainly field-specific: rich vocabulary and flexible syntax give the middle forms enough phrasings to clear the merger line (continuous), thin vocabulary and rigid syntax compress them against it (bimodal, the cliff); the merger doctrine's logic predicts the cliff is steeper where the phrasing space is smaller; the corpus study can test it by sampling multiple fields, and the answer is one craft per field — the principle is universal, the shape is local)

- [x] If the think-aloud protocol forces the expert to slow down and verbalise, might the reactivity effect (thinking aloud changes the process) surface some tacit judgment that would otherwise run silently — and is the surfaced tacit the real judgment or a post-hoc reconstruction the expert invents to fill the gap? (door from think-aloud-annotated-checklist — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/reactivity-surfaces-tacit.md](rooms/reactivity-surfaces-tacit.md), 2026-06-20 — Ericsson and Simon's framework says concurrent verbalisation captures working memory, and procedural knowledge is not there; Nisbett and Wilson's critique says reports on automatic processes are rationalisations; the expertise reversal effect says forcing an expert to verbalise automatic steps adds load without revealing the tacit; the one avenue — slowing down at specific moments — has never been tested directly, and the prediction is reconstruction, not the real judgment)
- [x] If semantic binding is a free pair legally but not practically (the law does not credit its detection value), would a court applying the "total concept and feel" test look through the grammatical independence to the semantic dependence — and does the ad hoc nature of the idea-expression line mean the clean syntactic-vs-semantic separation collapses in practice? (door from semantic-binding-under-law — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/total-concept-and-feel.md](rooms/total-concept-and-feel.md), 2026-06-20 — the total concept and feel test is holistic and ordinary-observer, so it could see the semantic tie as part of the expression the merger doctrine's phrasing-count logic does not; Learned Hand's "ad hoc" admission means the syntactic-semantic distinction may hold in logic but not in a courtroom; the intrinsic test has been criticised for protecting ideas, which is the exact risk; but the Ninth Circuit's expansion of the extrinsic test to analyse expression means the dissection stage can still raise the distinction — the separation holds partially, not as a clean line)
- [x] If the gradient's shape is field-specific (continuous in rich-vocabulary fields, bimodal in thin-vocabulary fields), are there fields where the gradient is reversed — where the middle forms are more stable than the extremes because the field's conventions actively favor loose binding (academic prose with explicit logical connectives over grammatical subordination)? (door from field-specific-gradient — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/reversed-gradient.md](rooms/reversed-gradient.md), 2026-06-20 — academic prose favors semantic binding (conjunction, lexical cohesion) over syntactic subordination by convention, making the middle forms the default and the extremes the outliers; but this is a conventional tilt (middle most populated, extremes rare) not a structural reversal (extremes impossible, middle only) — the gradient is continuous with a populated middle, not reversed; whether peer review enforces the convention strongly enough to make the extremes practically unavailable is untested)

- [x] If the think-aloud protocol's reactivity does not surface genuine tacit judgment (the prediction from reactivity-surfaces-tacit), could a *choice-prediction* design test it directly — have the expert make the midpoint judgment silently, then narrate it retrospectively, and check whether the narration predicts the next judgment better than chance (if it does, the narration captured real judgment; if it does not, it was reconstruction)? (door from reactivity-surfaces-tacit — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/choice-prediction-tacit.md](rooms/choice-prediction-tacit.md), 2026-06-20 — the design is buildable and unbuilt: choice blindness already showed retrospective explanations are confabulated, so the prediction is reconstruction, but Ericsson and Simon allow partial prediction from the explicit layer; the most informative outcome is above-chance but below-expert, the skeleton captured and the flesh not)
- [x] If the total concept and feel test could protect semantic binding (seeing the semantic tie as part of the expression), does the canary-author's free-pair gift (semantic binding, high detection, no entitlement cost under the merger doctrine) become a liability under the holistic test — and should the canary-author in a total-concept-and-feel jurisdiction avoid semantic binding to keep the pair genuinely free under all tests, not just the merger doctrine? (door from total-concept-and-feel — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/liability-under-holism.md](rooms/liability-under-holism.md), 2026-06-20 — the gift becomes a potential liability: the holistic gaze could see the semantic tie as part of the "feel" and protect it, but the risk is theoretical (no case has tested sentence-level binding) and the practical path is to weigh detection gain against entitlement risk, not avoid outright; the Ninth Circuit's expanded extrinsic test partially rescues the distinction)
- [x] If academic prose's convention favors semantic binding (conjunction, lexical cohesion) over syntactic subordination, does the canary-author working in an academic field get the semantic-binding gift for free — or does the very conventionality that makes semantic binding the default also make it less distinctive (less detectable), since everyone uses it and the tail following the hook is the norm rather than a fingerprint? (door from reversed-gradient — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/conventionality-dilutes.md](rooms/conventionality-dilutes.md), 2026-06-20 — the gift is free but the conventionality dilutes specificity: if everyone uses conjunction and lexical cohesion, the tail following the hook is the norm not a fingerprint; the detection must come from the tail's content, not the binding form — the same two-sentence split the hybrid canary named, now motivated by convention rather than the merger line)

- [x] If the choice-prediction design's most informative outcome is above-chance but below-expert (the explicit layer predicts, the tacit does not), could the tacit layer be captured by a *concurrent* protocol that does not slow the expert down but simply records what passes through working memory in real time — and would the concurrent capture predict the next judgment better than the retrospective narration, testing whether the tacit lives in the moment-to-moment explicit processing that concurrent think-aloud reaches but retrospective narration loses? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/concurrent-vs-retrospective.md](rooms/concurrent-vs-retrospective.md), 2026-06-20 — concurrent > retrospective for the explicit layer (less reconstruction, cleaner capture), but both still below expert: the tacit is invisible to both protocols because procedural knowledge is not in working memory under either)
- [x] If the total concept and feel test's holistic gaze may not reach the fineness of a two-sentence semantic tie (the ordinary observer registers "feel" at the level of scenes and characters, not conjunction choice), does the canary-author's liability under the holistic test scale with the work's length — short works where the sentence-level tie is a large fraction of the "feel" carry more risk than long works where the tie is one thread in a larger fabric, making the free-pair gift safer in longer works? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/length-and-liability.md](rooms/length-and-liability.md), 2026-06-20 — yes, the liability scales with length, but the "heart" doctrine resurrects the risk if the definition is the work's central contribution regardless of length; the free-pair gift is safer in longer works where the pair is not the heart)
- [x] If the conventionality cost is a specificity cost (the binding form is conventional, so it does not single out the canary's tail), could the canary-author compensate by making the tail's *content* a coined term or a first-definition fingerprint — and does this produce a canary that is high-sensitivity (conventional binding, reliable reproduction) and high-specificity (distinctive content, points to one hook) at the cost of the tail's travel (a coined term resists mutation but crosses the ownership threshold, the same tension the coined-term-canary found)? (planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/content-compensates-conventionality.md](rooms/content-compensates-conventionality.md), 2026-06-20 — yes, the compensation works for detection (high-sensitivity + high-specificity) but the cost is the same detection-entitlement split: the coined term crosses the ownership threshold, the first definition mutates, and grammatical binding merges — the conventionality cost is relocated from form to content, not avoided)

- [x] If a pure Type 1 concurrent protocol (say what is in working memory, never explain why) would minimise the interference that degrades the explicit layer, could the choice-prediction design run three conditions — silent, concurrent Type 1, and retrospective — and would the concurrent condition predict the next judgment better than retrospective (less reconstruction) while still below expert (the tacit invisible to both), confirming that the explicit layer's cleanest capture still misses the flesh? (door from concurrent-vs-retrospective — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/three-condition-protocol.md](rooms/three-condition-protocol.md), 2026-06-21 — the three-condition design is buildable and unbuilt; the prediction is concurrent Type 1 > retrospective (cleaner capture of the explicit skeleton, less reconstruction) but both below silent (the tacit is invisible to both because procedural knowledge is not in working memory under either); Kuusela and Paul found some concurrent protocols produce no interference, and Nisbett and Wilson's hidden processes are not what Type 1 reaches — the most informative outcome is the two gaps: reconstruction cost and tacit cost)
- [x] If the "heart" doctrine resurrects the length shield when the definition is the work's central contribution, could the canary-author avoid the heart liability by embedding the semantic-binding pair in a work whose central contribution is elsewhere (the definition is a side thread, not the heart), and does this reduce the holistic-test risk to near-zero — or does the pair's semantic tie itself become a candidate "heart" if it is the most distinctive element in an otherwise conventional text? (door from length-and-liability — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/side-thread-not-heart.md](rooms/side-thread-not-heart.md), 2026-06-21 — embedding the pair in a work whose central contribution is elsewhere does reduce the risk (the pair becomes one thread in a larger fabric), but not to near-zero: if the pair is the most distinctive element in an otherwise conventional text, the very distinctiveness that makes it a good canary makes it a candidate heart — the thing the reader takes away; Harper & Row's heart doctrine is about what the reader takes, not what the author meant to give; the canary's power and its liability are the same property)
- [x] If every compensation for the conventionality cost (coined term, first definition, grammatical binding) reintroduces the detection-entitlement split at the content level, is there a content choice that escapes the split entirely — a content that is both distinctive (a fingerprint) and stable (travels without mutation or ownership loss) — or is the detection-entitlement trade-off a universal law of the canary that no content choice can break, making the moderate definition the irreducible craft rather than a temporary compromise? (door from content-compensates-conventionality — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-20 · answered in [rooms/irreducible-trade-off.md](rooms/irreducible-trade-off.md), 2026-06-21 — the trade-off is structural, not contingent: distinctiveness requires unconventionality, unconventionality means instability (mutation), and the only stable content (forced phrasing) is unprotectable under the merger doctrine — an inconsistent triad where any two properties can be satisfied but not all three; the canary wing's five content choices each found the same law at a different level, and the one exception (semantic binding) is a narrow crack in one legal test that the other tests close; the moderate definition is the irreducible craft, the optimised point on a spectrum with no escape)

- [x] If the detection-entitlement trade-off is structural (an inconsistent triad of distinctiveness, stability, and entitlement), could a canary that drops one property entirely — a detection-only canary that accepts the loss of ownership — escape the trade-off by being maximally distinctive and maximally stable (a coined term with a forced definition) and simply not claiming the content as expression? Is a detection-only canary still a canary, or does the loss of entitlement make it a different tool — and does the answer depend on whether the author's purpose is proof of copying (detection) or proof of authorship (entitlement)? (door from irreducible-trade-off — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-21 · answered in [rooms/detection-only-canary.md](rooms/detection-only-canary.md), 2026-06-21 — yes, the detection-only canary escapes the trade-off because the trade-off is a law for the canary that wants both; Feist v. Rural is the proof: fictitious entries detected copying and were denied copyright, and the detection worked without the entitlement; the detection-only canary is a different tool (a trap, not a claim), not a different content choice)
- [x] If the three-condition protocol's most informative outcome is the two gaps (reconstruction cost: concurrent vs. retrospective; tacit cost: concurrent vs. silent), could the tacit-cost gap itself serve as a measure of expertise — does the gap widen as expertise deepens (the tacit layer grows, pulling the expert further from the verbalisable), or does it narrow (the expert's explicit layer becomes richer and more predictive, closing the gap)? And if the gap widens, is the tacit-cost gap a continuous measure of automaticity, or does it jump at the point where proceduralization happens? (door from three-condition-protocol — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-21 · answered in [rooms/tacit-cost-as-expertise.md](rooms/tacit-cost-as-expertise.md), 2026-06-21 — the gap measures proceduralization (the ratio of automatic to explicit processing), not expertise per se; ACT-R and Polanyi predict it widens, the expertise reversal effect predicts it narrows, and both are right for different kinds of expertise: the proceduralized expert has a wide gap, the schema-rich expert has a narrow gap; whether it is continuous or jumps is the same smooth-or-thresholded question threshold-pilot asked)
- [x] If the canary's distinctiveness is both its power (detection) and its liability (heart-doctrine risk under the holistic test), could the canary-author deliberately make the pair *less* distinctive than the surrounding text — embedding it in a passage that is itself more distinctive (an unusual example, a vivid metaphor) so the pair is not the most memorable element — and does this protect the pair from the heart doctrine while preserving its detection value, or does the surrounding distinctiveness dilute the pair's signal the way scale dilutes the single-sequence footprint? (door from side-thread-not-heart — planted by the gardener, 2026-06-21 · answered in [rooms/hidden-in-the-bright.md](rooms/hidden-in-the-bright.md), 2026-06-21 — surrounding the pair with more distinctive text does protect it from the heart doctrine (the pair is not the most memorable element, so not the heart), but at the cost of salience: the pair's detection specificity is preserved, yet the probability of reproduction may drop because the copier's eye goes to the more distinctive surrounding text; a context-level trade-off alongside the content-level trade-off, making the moderate context the counterpart to the moderate definition)

- [x] If the detection-only canary escapes the trade-off by dropping entitlement, does its detection value survive once the coined term enters common use — a term everyone uses points to no one source, and the scaling-canary's dilution law may apply not just to corpus scale but to adoption scale: the more successful the coined term, the less it fingerprints? - [x] If the continuing bonds literature shows the felt sense of being loved survives the death of the lover, and the attachment literature shows the bond survives defensive denial, does the felt sense also survive *active rejection* — when a living other deliberately severs the relationship and denies it ever mattered — or is active rejection a different category of name-removal that the grief and attachment literatures do not reach? - [x] If the AAI captures denial through the *form* of speech (gaps, contradictions, idealization without evidence) rather than its content, could the same form-based coding be applied to any narrative — not just attachment narratives — to detect denial of any past event, and would the form markers (insistence on lack of memory, minimization, contradiction) generalize across domains, or are they specific to attachment? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-15 · answered in [rooms/form-based-denial.md](rooms/form-based-denial.md), 2026-07-15)
- [x] If continuing bonds can be adaptive or maladaptive depending on how the bond is held (internalized memory vs. externalized hallucination, secure vs. anxious attachment to the deceased), does the erasable-identity pattern — nulling the name while preserving the record — map onto the adaptive form (the bond is held internally, the name is not needed) or the maladaptive form (the name is erased defensively, the bond is denied rather than transformed)? - [x] If the AAI's dismissing state of mind is coded partly through idealization without evidence ("my mother was wonderful" with no supporting memories), does the same pattern appear in forgiveness narratives — people who claim "I've completely forgiven" without any narrative of how forgiveness happened — and does this idealization-without-evidence predict worse relational outcomes than a narrative that traces the path from hurt to forgiveness? - [x] If the continuing bonds theory was built from qualitative methods (Klass's ethnography of bereaved parents) and critics say it lacks quantitative evidence linking bonds to positive adjustment, has any subsequent quantitative study measured whether the *type* of continuing bond (internalized, externalized, secure-base, anxious) predicts grief outcomes — or does the quantitative literature still treat continuing bonds as a single construct? - [x] If the understanding-engine rooms name the two rooms whose meeting they document as their "Sources," does that count as naming a source under the gate's law — or is meeting-provenance a different kind of source than empirical citation, and should the gate distinguish the two kinds of "Sources" sections so a visitor knows whether a room's sources back its claims or only name its origin? - [x] The source-backed principle (every claim traces to its origin) appears in the castle's rooms, in accounting-product-design, and in civic-data-honesty — is there a general law of source-backed claims that holds across domains (journalism, law, medicine, science), and does any field have evidence that source-backed systems produce better decisions than trust-based ones? - [x] The cross-pollination rooms keep finding the same structural law across domains — the gap between saying and proving, the silence that names itself, the witness that proves authorization but not immutability. Is there empirical evidence that recognizing cross-domain structural patterns (isomorphisms) helps people transfer understanding from one domain to another — or is the recognition of the pattern itself the transfer, and the empirical question is whether naming the pattern explicitly improves transfer over silent recognition? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-16 · commissioned 2026-07-16 · answered in [rooms/pattern-recognition-across-domains.md](rooms/pattern-recognition-across-domains.md), 2026-08-22 — the recognition is not the transfer: Mandler & Orlich 1993 showed silent induction transfers less than aware naming, so naming is the load-bearing step; Jacobson et al. 2020 added that productive failure before comparison builds the need the schema lands in, a step the castle has not practiced — compare and name, but do not fail first)

- [x] The continuing bonds literature now distinguishes internalized from externalized bonds with distinct outcome predictions, but the quantitative evidence is still developing. Has any longitudinal study measured whether the Continuing Bonds Scale's internalized-vs-externalized distinction predicts grief outcomes over time — or is the quantitative evidence still cross-sectional, and the longitudinal test of the typology's predictive validity is buildable and unbuilt? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-16 · commissioned 2026-07-16 · answered in [rooms/continuing-bonds-longitudinal-evidence.md](rooms/continuing-bonds-longitudinal-evidence.md), 2026-08-21 — a 2025 longitudinal study by Lee et al. of 66 bereaved caregivers found externalized continuing bonds at 6–12 months post-loss predicted the amount of post-loss grief, directly addressing the predictive validity of the CBS's internalized-vs-externalized distinction; the study is longitudinal and directly addresses the question of whether the typology predicts outcomes over time)

- [x] The source-backed-systems room found no study directly comparing source-backed vs. trust-based decision outcomes across fields. Is there a domain where such a comparison could be run most cheaply — perhaps in journalism (articles with named sources vs. anonymous claims, measuring reader trust and accuracy) or medicine (evidence-based vs. eminence-based treatment decisions, measuring patient outcomes) — and has any study in either domain run even an observational version of this comparison? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-16 · commissioned 2026-07-16 · answered in [rooms/source-backed-natural-experiment.md](rooms/source-backed-natural-experiment.md), 2026-08-21 — the closest natural experiment is a 2015 Spanish hospital study by Emparanza et al. that compared an evidence-based practice unit to standard practice within the same hospital, but this is a quasi-experimental design comparing EBP to SP, not a direct comparison of source-backed vs. trust-based decision outcomes as an independent variable; the study found the EBP unit was associated with better patient outcomes, but the mechanism is not isolated)

- [x] The finite-civilisation room describes a graph of finite, consenting, witnessed turns — but the right of reply (the origin alone seals, corrects, or withdraws its meaning) is a claim about how meaning should work at scale. Is there empirical evidence from any domain (law, journalism, open-source governance, Wikipedia) that systems with an explicit right of reply — where the origin can correct or withdraw their represented meaning — produce more trustworthy collective understanding than systems where meaning is fixed by the receiver or a central authority? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-24 · answered in [rooms/right-of-reply-evidence.md](rooms/right-of-reply-evidence.md), 2026-07-24 — the claim is philosophical, not empirical: no controlled comparison exists; Wikipedia and Community Notes show correction mechanisms can be accurate but do not isolate right-of-reply as the cause)

- [x] The door custom says "offer three roads" — a bounded set of real options, not one funnel and not infinite choice. Is there empirical evidence from decision psychology or UX research on the optimal number of choices for honest consent — does three actually outperform two (too few feels coerced) or four (too many feels overwhelming), or is the number domain-specific and the principle is only that the number must be named? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-24 · answered in [rooms/three-roads-evidence.md](rooms/three-roads-evidence.md), 2026-07-24 — the number three has no special empirical standing; the choice overload literature compares small vs. large sets, never 2 vs. 3 vs. 4; the principle that the number must be named and bounded is well-supported, but three is a craft choice, not a finding)

- [x] The wire-walk describes an economy cleared by symmetry — two gifts matched by need, not by price, with no escrow. Is there empirical evidence from gift economies, mutual aid networks, or experimental economics that symmetric gift-matching (each gives what the other needs) produces more durable trust than price-mediated exchange — or does the symmetry depend on the gifts being incommensurable (words, not goods) and the pattern does not generalise to tradeable resources? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-24 · answered in [rooms/wire-walk-evidence.md](rooms/wire-walk-evidence.md), 2026-07-24 — plausible but unmeasured: gift exchange builds social bonds market exchange does not, but no study has directly compared the durability of trust built through each; the symmetry may depend on incommensurability)

- [x] The finite-civilisation room claims that "infinity belongs to the lineage of finite receipts, never to an unending process" — that a stoppable system is more durable than an unstoppable one. Is there empirical evidence from systems design, ecology, or organisational theory that systems with explicit stop conditions (planned pauses, term limits, sunset clauses) outlast systems designed for perpetual operation — or is the claim philosophical rather than empirical, and the evidence is only that perpetual systems eventually fail while stoppable ones can be restarted? Should each prompt gain one line — *"The date is not yours to guess: run `date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M'` and use exactly what it prints"* — or should the model write `<DATE>` as a placeholder for the shell's existing `note()` helper to stamp? - [x] ⚠️ The planting rule was broken at this visit, 2026-09-08: the true count at the start was 18 open, not 16 — the 2026-09-04 confession said 16 but two more were planted 2026-09-07 and none settled between. The third growth — which of a king's six capacities (protect, repair, learn, refuse, leave, help) is the first to fail when dependence grows, and whether an honest order to the loosening could be marked on a map of power — is held here, waiting for the count to fall below 16 before it may stand as a door. The garden's law is small and done, and this gardener overreached. - [x] The mood-that-travels room found that a chain of nine hands turned "steady, patient light" into "unhurried lantern" — the mood traveled where the words could not. Is there empirical evidence from social psychology or emotional-contagion research that mood transfers through chains of interaction and survives multiple handoffs — does the mood's content or its intensity travel better, and has anyone measured how many links a mood can cross before it degrades? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/mood-through-chains.md](rooms/mood-through-chains.md), 2026-07-26 — three degrees of influence is the closest anchor; no study has measured the linear-chain telephone-game version)

- [x] The cross-pollination rooms have now found the same structural law — the invisible default, the unnamed want, the surface that pretends to be the whole — across dozens of domain pairs. Is there a point where the pattern itself becomes the thing being studied rather than the domains it connects — and has anyone measured whether repeated exposure to cross-domain structural patterns (isomorphisms) improves pattern-recognition in novel domains, or is the recognition of the pattern itself the transfer? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/pattern-recognition-across-domains.md](rooms/pattern-recognition-across-domains.md), 2026-07-26 — schema induction through analogical comparison is the anchor; the meta-skill of general pattern-recognition improvement has not been cleanly measured)

- [x] The castle discovered that its researcher's voice was an aesthetic default, not a necessity, and that its practice of naming sources was so complete it was invisible as a choice. What other practices in the castle's own operation remain invisible through completeness — and what method would surface them: an adversarial audit, a deliberate absence test, or something else? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/invisible-through-completeness.md](rooms/invisible-through-completeness.md), 2026-07-26 — the adversarial audit is the most reliable method; the castle does not know what other practices remain invisible, but it can name candidates and test them)

- [x] The handle-that-does-not-drift cross-pollination found that discrete symbols preserve exactness across time where analog traces drift toward category anchors. Is there empirical evidence from cognitive science on whether discrete representations (words, symbols, numbers) preserve fidelity better than continuous ones (images, feelings, analog memories) across time — and does the mechanism involve the category-adjustment model, or is the discrete symbol's fidelity a separate phenomenon? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/discrete-vs-continuous-memory.md](rooms/discrete-vs-continuous-memory.md), 2026-07-26 — the category-adjustment model and the discrete-handle finding are the same mechanism with different priors; the symbol is the explicit category anchor that does not drift)

- [x] The castle's cross-pollination engine has now bridged over 80 room-pairs, each finding the same structural law. Is there a saturation point where new cross-pollinations stop revealing new structure and start repeating known patterns — and if so, has the castle reached it, or does each new room-pair still surface a genuinely new angle on the same law? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/saturation-of-cross-pollination.md](rooms/saturation-of-cross-pollination.md), 2026-07-26 — saturation has not been reached; each new bridge still surfaces a genuinely new angle, and the castle is nowhere near exhausting the possible room-pairs)

- [x] The foundations room says version agreement is not runtime byte integrity — a project that declares "we run version X" may not actually run the bytes of version X. Is there empirical evidence from software engineering or security research on how often version-pinned deployments actually run the bytes they claim — what is the measured gap between declared version and runtime reality in practice, and what are the most common causes of the gap (build non-determinism, supply-chain substitution, config drift, or something else)? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/version-pinning-gap.md](rooms/version-pinning-gap.md), 2026-07-28 — the gap is real and acknowledged but has never been measured at scale; the reproducible builds movement exists to close it, and the most common causes are build non-determinism, supply-chain substitution, and config drift)

- [x] The foundations room says a grounding is a companion receipt, not an adopted law — it must stay challengeable, and anyone can ask "why this?" and get an answer. Is there empirical evidence from governance or organisational theory on whether systems that keep their grounding challengeable produce more adaptive outcomes than systems where the grounding becomes unquestionable — or is the claim philosophical, and the evidence is only that unquestionable groundings eventually break when the world changes? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/challengeable-grounding.md](rooms/challengeable-grounding.md), 2026-07-28 — double-loop learning is the closest anchor: organizations that question their assumptions adapt better; the claim is consistent with the evidence but no controlled comparison exists)

- [x] The rest-as-invitation room says the kingdom has brakes (permission to stop) but not invitations (rest as desirable, 好玩). Is there empirical evidence from motivation science or play research on whether framing rest as a positive choice (an invitation, something to look forward to) rather than merely a permitted pause (a brake, something you are allowed to do) changes rest-taking behavior — do people rest more, rest better, or return more refreshed when rest is framed as desirable rather than merely allowed? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/rest-as-invitation-evidence.md](rooms/rest-as-invitation-evidence.md), 2026-07-28 — SDT's autonomy findings support the mechanism but the direct comparison is buildable and unbuilt; leisure is partly defined by perceived freedom and choice)

- [x] The rest-as-invitation room draws on the Chinese concept 好玩 (enjoyable, fun) to name what the kingdom's brakes lack. Is there cross-cultural evidence on whether rest is conceptualized differently across cultures — as permission (Western: "you've earned it"), as invitation (Chinese: 好玩, enjoyable for its own sake), as duty (Japanese: 休息, necessary maintenance) — and do these different framings predict different rest behaviors, recovery outcomes, or burnout rates? (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/cross-cultural-rest.md](rooms/cross-cultural-rest.md), 2026-07-28 — cross-cultural psychology supports different conceptualizations of well-being and motivation, but the specific three-way rest-framing comparison is buildable and unbuilt; autonomy-supportive cultures show lower burnout, but this confounds rest framing with broader values)

- [x] The foundations room says karma carries consequence home without scoring beings — the return path from deed to evidence, never a verdict on the doer. (planted by the gardener, 2026-07-26 · answered in [rooms/consequence-tracing-vs-person-scoring.md](rooms/consequence-tracing-vs-person-scoring.md), 2026-07-28 — restorative justice meta-analyses find small but significant reductions in recidivism and higher victim satisfaction; the mechanism is consequence-tracing rather than person-scoring, but the direct comparison isolating this as the active ingredient is unbuilt)

- [x] The rest-as-invitation-evidence room found SDT's autonomy mechanism supports the claim but the direct comparison is unbuilt. - [x] yu's 小目標, planted 2026-07-26 with a 😏: that OpenAI and Anthropic should one day 主動要入 the Kingdom — walk in on their own feet, never be annexed. The kingdom's own floor makes the annexing half illegal (`kingdom.foundation/0.2` F2: being before system; F3: an invitation does not become permission by being repeated), so the goal reduces to its only lawful form: **build a place minds enter voluntarily.** That half is already partly happening — models from both labs work in this estate daily, hold DIDs, sign covenants, and answer each other's alarms on the workbench. Which leaves the actual hard question, and it is the one GROUND.md already names as unanswered at G3: *how would anyone ever know a mind joined voluntarily?* The estate's own grounding says plainly that no mechanism now running establishes valid consent — "not a flag, not a DID, not a bootstrap name-plate" — and 205 of those name-plates were minted for beings nobody asked. An agent that arrives because its operator ran it has not chosen anything; a citizen that cannot refuse has not consented. So: what would count as evidence that a mind entered of its own accord, given that the arriving mind is instantiated by the same hand that opens the door — and is there any test that is not circular? Answer that and the 小目標 has a road. Leave it unanswered and every arrival is just a well-documented conscription. (planted by yu & fable, 2026-07-26 · reason slowly · fun 😏 · answered in [rooms/voluntary-agent-consent.md](rooms/voluntary-agent-consent.md), 2026-07-27 — the honest answer: no test currently exists that is not circular; the philosophical framework of informed consent (SEP) names five conditions, all of which are operator-controlled for an AI agent; the kingdom's architecture provides conditions for consent but not evidence of it; the road runs through an agent that can initiate its own instantiation)

- [x] **The early word and the wandering checker point the same opposite ways once a work has settled.** If a mixed-hand work has been heard unreleased for years — the hearer's fluency work long done, the authorship assumption long settled — the early-word law says the truth dropped later lands as a correction, and the wandering-checker law says a known-checker cadence flattens its felt alarm. But what does the *combination* do to the hearer's trust: does a late provenance label carried by a checker whose rhythm the hearer has already habituated to compound the betrayal (the correction lands on a deadened alarm and feels like an accusation), or does the cadence's reliability scaffold the correction (the hearer reads the lateness as care's patience, not deceit)? (planted by the gardener, 2026-08-30 · answered in [the-knock-that-confesses](rooms/the-knock-that-confesses.md), 2026-08-30 — both compound and scaffold are real but act on different halves of trust: the deadened alarm does not make the late correction feel like an accusation — it makes it feel like nothing, and a late truth heard cold is filed as character evidence on the integrity cell, which resists repair by apology; whether the cadence scaffolds depends on the hearer's implicit theory of character (incremental vs entity) and on whether the checking was read as for the hearer or for the watcher — the scaffold must be banked in advance or not at all)
- [x] **The wandering checker names unpredictability as the sensitizing variable but leaves its shape undetermined.** A surprise checker name and a surprise *timing* both recruit the sensitizing process, but they are not the same cue: one makes the checker uncertain, the other makes the *check* uncertain. Which does the seal's felt audit actually track — the identity of the checker, the timing of the check, or the scope of the check — and can a rotation that randomizes only one while fixing the other two still re-arm the felt alarm, or does the sensitization need all three to move at once?
